When due to their personal circumstances they cannot immediately repair the damage caused, they shall provide security or submit to the measures that in the judge's or court's opinion are sufficient to ensure that they will fulfill this obligation within the time period set for them.  
III.-	The suspension shall include the prison sentence and the fine, and as to the other sanctions imposed, the judge or court shall resolve at their discretion according to the circumstances of the case.  

IV.-  Offenders for whom the execution of the sentence has been suspended shall be informed of the provisions of this article, which shall be recorded in a formal proceeding, without the absence of this preventing, as applicable, the application of what is provided therein.  
V.-  Sentenced persons who enjoy the benefits of conditional sentencing shall be subject to the care and supervision of the General Directorate of Coordinated Services for Prevention and Social Rehabilitation.  
VI.- In the event that a guarantor has been appointed for the fulfillment of the obligations undertaken under the terms of this article, the guarantor's obligation shall conclude six months after the expiration of the term referred to in section VII, provided that the offender does not give rise to new proceedings or when an acquittal is pronounced therein. When the guarantor has well-founded reasons not to continue performing the position, they shall present them to the judge so that the judge, if they deem them just, may require the sentenced person to present a new guarantor within the time period that should be prudently set, with the warning that the sanction will be enforced if they do not do so. In the event of death or insolvency of the guarantor, the sentenced person shall be obligated to bring the fact to the judge's attention for the purpose and under the warning expressed in the preceding paragraph.  
Section amended DOF 30-12-1991

VII.- If during the term of duration of the penalty, from the date of the sentence that becomes final, the convicted person does not give rise to new proceedings for an intentional crime that concludes with a conviction sentence, the sanction set forth therein shall be considered extinguished. Otherwise, the first sentence shall be enforced, in addition to the second, in which the defendant shall be recorded as a repeat offender without prejudice to what is established in article 20 of this Code. In the case of a negligent crime, the competent authority shall resolve with reasoning whether or not the suspended sanction should be applied;  
Section amended DOF 30-12-1991, 10-01-1994

VIII.- The facts that give rise to the new proceedings interrupt the term referred to in section VII whether it involves an intentional or negligent crime, until a final sentence is issued;  
Section amended DOF 30-12-1991, 10-01-1994

IX.- In the event of failure to fulfill the obligations undertaken by the convicted person, the judge may enforce the suspended sanction or admonish them, with the warning that, if they again fail to comply with any of the conditions set, said sanction shall be enforced.  
X.-  The defendant who considers that when the sentence was issued they met the conditions set forth in this provision and that they are able to fulfill the other requirements established, if it is due to inadvertence on their part or on the part of the courts that they did not obtain the granting of conditional sentencing in the sentence, may request that it be granted to them, opening the respective incidental proceeding before the trial judge.  
Article amended DOF 19-03-1971

#### CHAPTER V

Transparency in the benefits of early release or conditional sentencing
Chapter added DOF 23-01-2009

##### Article 90 Bis.- Repealed

Article added DOF 23-01-2009. Repealed DOF 17-06-2016

TITLE FIVE

On the Causes of Extinction of Criminal Action
Title denomination amended DOF 17-06-2016

#### CHAPTER I

Death of the accused or sentenced person
Chapter denomination amended DOF 17-06-2016

##### Article 91

The death of the accused extinguishes the criminal action, as well as the sanctions that may have been imposed upon them, with the exception of the repair of damage, precautionary measures, seizure and the forfeiture of the instruments, objects or products of the crime as well as the assets whose value is equivalent to said product in accordance with the provisions of article 40 of this Code.  
Article amended DOF 17-06-2016

#### CHAPTER II

Amnesty

##### Article 92

Amnesty extinguishes the criminal action and the sanctions imposed, except the repair of damage, under the terms of the law that is issued granting it, and if they are not expressed, it shall be understood that the criminal action and the sanctions imposed are extinguished with all their effects, in relation to all those responsible for the crime.

#### CHAPTER III

Pardon of the Victim or Person Authorized to Grant It
Chapter denomination amended DOF 13-01-1984

##### Article 93

The pardon of the victim or of the person authorized to grant it may only be granted when all damages and losses caused by the commission of the crime have been repaired; this extinguishes the criminal action with respect to crimes that are prosecuted by complaint, provided that it is granted before the Public Prosecutor's Office if the latter has not exercised the same or before the jurisdictional body before a second instance judgment is issued. Once the pardon is granted, it may not be revoked.  
Paragraph reformed DOF 14-06-2012

The provisions of the preceding paragraph are equally applicable to crimes that can only be prosecuted by declaration of prejudice or by some other act equivalent to a complaint, the manifestation by the person authorized to do so that the affected interest has been satisfied being sufficient for the extinction of the criminal action.  
When there are several victims and each one may separately exercise the power to pardon the person responsible for the crime and the concealer, the pardon shall only have effect with respect to the person who grants it.  

The pardon only benefits the accused in whose favor it is granted, unless the victim or the person authorized to grant it has obtained satisfaction of their interests or rights, in which case it shall benefit all the accused and the concealer.  
Paragraph reformed DOF 17-06-2016 Errata to the article DOF 31-08-1931. Reformed DOF 13-01-1984, 10-01-1994, 04-12-2002

#### CHAPTER IV

Recognition of Innocence and Pardon
Title of the Chapter reformed DOF 13-01-1984

##### Article 94

Pardon may not be granted except for a sanction imposed in an irrevocable judgment.

##### Article 95

It may not be granted for disqualification from exercising a profession or any civil or political rights, or from holding a certain position or employment, as these sanctions shall only be extinguished by amnesty or rehabilitation.

##### Article 96

When it appears that the convicted person is innocent, recognition of their innocence shall proceed, in the terms provided by the applicable Code of Criminal Procedure, and the provisions of Article 49 of this Code shall apply.  
Article reformed DOF 13-01-1984

##### Article 97

When the conduct observed by the convicted person reflects a high degree of social reintegration and their release does not represent a risk to public tranquility and security, according to the opinion of the executing body of the sanction, and it is not a person convicted of treason against the Nation, espionage, terrorism, sabotage, genocide, crimes against health, rape, intentional crime against life and kidnapping, forced disappearance, torture and trafficking in persons, nor a repeat offender for an intentional crime, pardon may be granted by the Federal Executive, in the exercise of discretionary powers, expressing its reasons and grounds in the following cases:
Paragraph reformed DOF 17-06-2016

I.-	For crimes of a political nature referred to in Article 144 of this Code;  

II.-	For other crimes when the conduct of those responsible has been determined by motivations of a political or social nature, and
III.-	For crimes of federal jurisdiction or common crimes in the Federal District, when the convicted person has rendered important services to the Nation, and upon request.  
Article reformed DOF 31-10-1989

##### Article 97

Bis.- Exceptionally, on its own initiative or at the request of the Plenary of either of the Chambers of the Congress of the Union, the Head of the Federal Executive Branch may grant pardon for any crime of federal jurisdiction or common crime in the Federal District, and subject to an opinion from the executing body of the sanction demonstrating that the convicted person does not represent a danger to public tranquility and security, expressing its reasons and grounds, when there are consistent indications of serious violations of the human rights of the convicted person.  

The Federal Executive must ensure that the convicted person has previously exhausted all national legal remedies.  
Article added DOF 30-10-2013

##### Article 98

Pardon shall in no case extinguish the obligation to repair the damage caused. Recognition of the innocence of the convicted person extinguishes the obligation to repair the damage.  
Article reformed DOF 13-01-1984, 31-10-1989

#### CHAPTER V

Rehabilitation

##### Article 99

Rehabilitation has the purpose of restoring to the convicted person the civil, political or family rights that they had lost by virtue of a judgment issued in a proceeding or in the exercise of which they were suspended.  
Article reformed DOF 17-06-2016

#### CHAPTER VI

Statute of Limitations

##### Article 100

By statute of limitations, the criminal action and the sanctions are extinguished, in accordance with the following articles.

##### Article 101

The statute of limitations is personal and for it the simple passage of time established by law shall suffice.  
The periods for the statute of limitations shall be doubled with respect to those who are outside the national territory, if due to this circumstance it is not possible to conduct an investigation, conclude a proceeding or execute a sanction.  
Paragraph added DOF 13-01-1984. Reformed DOF 17-06-2016

The statute of limitations shall produce its effect, even if the accused, defendant and convicted person does not allege it as a defense. The jurisdictional body shall apply it ex officio in all cases, as soon as it becomes aware of it, whatever the state of the proceeding.  
Paragraph reformed DOF 17-06-2016

##### Article 102

The periods for the statute of limitations of the criminal action shall be continuous; in them the crime shall be considered with its modalities, and they shall be counted:
Paragraph reformed DOF 23-12-1985

I.-	From the moment the crime was consummated, if it is instantaneous;  

II.-	From the day the last act of execution was performed or the required conduct was omitted, if the crime is in the stage of attempt;  
III.-	From the day the last conduct was performed, in the case of a continuing crime; and
IV.-	From the cessation of consummation in a permanent crime.  
Article reformed DOF 13-01-1984

##### Article 103

The periods for the statute of limitations of sanctions shall be equally continuous and shall run from the day following that on which the convicted person evades the action of justice, if the sanctions are deprivations or restrictions of liberty, and if they are not, from the date of the final judgment.  
Article reformed DOF 23-12-1985

##### Article 104

The criminal action prescribes in one year, if the crime only merits a fine; if the crime merits, in addition to this sanction, a custodial sentence or alternative sentence, the statute of limitations of the action to prosecute the custodial sentence shall be considered; the same shall be observed when any other accessory sanction is to be imposed.  
Article reformed DOF 23-12-1985

##### Article 105

The criminal action shall prescribe in a period equal to the arithmetic mean of the custodial sentence established by law for the crime in question, but in no case shall it be less than three years.  
Article reformed DOF 23-12-1985

##### Article 106

The criminal action shall prescribe in two years, if the crime only merits removal from office, suspension, deprivation of rights or disqualification, except as provided in other regulations.  
Article reformed DOF 23-12-1985

##### Article 107

When the law does not provide otherwise, the criminal action arising from an offense that can only be prosecuted by complaint of the victim or some other equivalent act, shall prescribe in one year, counted from the day on which those who may file the complaint or the equivalent act, have knowledge of the offense and of the offender, and in three, outside of this circumstance.  
Errata to the paragraph DOF 31-08-1931. Amended DOF 23-12-1985

But once the procedural requirement has been fulfilled within the aforementioned period, the prescription shall continue to run according to the rules for offenses prosecutable ex officio.  
Errata to the paragraph DOF 31-08-1931. Amended DOF 10-01-1994

##### Article 107

Bis.- The prescription period for the offenses provided for in Title Eight of Book Two of this Code committed against a minor victim, shall begin to run from the time the victim reaches the age of majority.  
In the case of those persons who do not have the capacity to understand the meaning of the act or of persons who do not have the capacity to resist it, it shall run from the moment there is evidence of the commission of those offenses before the Public Prosecutor.  

In the cases of offenses against freedom and normal psychosexual development, as well as those provided for in the Law to Prevent and Punish Trafficking in Persons, that have been committed against a person under eighteen years of age, the rules for the prescription of criminal action contained in this chapter shall be observed, but the commencement of the calculation of the periods shall begin from the day on which the victim reaches the age of majority.  
Paragraph added DOF 14-06-2012 Article added DOF 19-08-2010

##### Article 108

In cases of concurrence of offenses, the criminal actions resulting from them shall prescribe when that of the offense that merits the greater penalty prescribes.  
Errata to the article DOF 31-08-1931. Amended DOF 23-12-1985

##### Article 109

When a prior resolution of jurisdictional authority is necessary to exercise or continue the criminal action, the prescription shall begin to run from the date the final judgment is issued.  
Article amended DOF 23-12-1985

##### Article 110

The prescription of actions shall be interrupted by the proceedings carried out in the investigation and of the accused, even if due to ignorance of who they are the proceedings are not carried out against a specific person.  
Paragraph amended DOF 17-06-2016

If action ceases, the prescription shall begin to run again from the day following that of the last proceeding.  
The prescription of actions shall also be interrupted by the request for assistance in the investigation of the offense or of who has committed it or participated in its commission, by the proceedings carried out to obtain international extradition, and by the request for surrender of the accused formally made by the Public Prosecutor of one federative entity to that of another where the accused takes refuge, is located or is detained for the same or for another offense. In the first case the proceedings carried out by the requested authority shall also cause the interruption and in the second the interruption shall subsist until the requested authority denies the surrender or until the legal situation of the detainee disappears, which gives reason for the postponement of the surrender.  
Paragraph added DOF 10-01-1994. Amended DOF 17-06-2016

The interruption of the prescription of the criminal action may only extend up to one half the periods indicated in articles 105, 106 and 107 of this Code.  
Paragraph added DOF 10-01-1994 Errata to the article DOF 31-08-1931. Amended DOF 23-12-1985

##### Article 111

The provisions contained in the first two paragraphs and in the first case of the third paragraph of the preceding article, shall not operate when the proceedings are carried out after half of the period necessary for prescription has elapsed.  

The period that article 107 establishes for the complaint or other equivalent requirement to be satisfied is excepted from the preceding rule.  
Article amended DOF 15-01-1951, 10-01-1994

##### Article 112

If to bring a criminal action the law requires a prior declaration or resolution of some authority, the steps taken for that purpose, before the period indicated in the preceding article, shall interrupt the prescription.  
Article amended DOF 15-01-1951, 23-12-1985

##### Article 113

Unless the law provides otherwise, the custodial penalty shall prescribe in a time equal to that fixed in the sentence and one quarter more, but it may not be less than three years; the fine penalty shall prescribe in one year; the other sanctions shall prescribe in a period equal to that which they should last and one quarter more, without it being able to be less than two years; those that do not have temporality shall prescribe in two years. The periods shall be counted from the date on which the resolution becomes final.  
Article amended DOF 23-12-1985

##### Article 114

When the sentenced person has already served part of the sanction, as much time as remains of the sentence and one quarter more shall be necessary for prescription, but it may not be less than one year.  
Article amended DOF 23-12-1985, 17-06-2016

##### Article 115

The prescription of the custodial sanction is only interrupted by apprehending the accused even if the apprehension is executed for another different offense, or by the formal request for surrender that the Public Prosecutor of one federative entity makes to that of another in which the accused is detained, in which case the interruption shall subsist until the requested authority denies said surrender or the legal situation of the detainee that motivates postponing compliance with what was requested disappears.  
Paragraph amended DOF 17-06-2016

The prescription of the other sanctions shall be interrupted by any act of competent authority to make them effective. The prescription of the penalty of reparation of damage or of others of a pecuniary nature shall also be interrupted by the motions that the victim or person in whose favor said reparation has been decreed makes before the corresponding fiscal authority and by the proceedings that said authority carries out to execute them, as well as by the commencement of executory proceedings before civil authority using the corresponding conviction as title.  
Article amended DOF 23-12-1985, 10-01-1994

#### CHAPTER VII

Fulfillment of the Penalty or Security Measure
Chapter added DOF 13-01-1984. Relocated with amended denomination DOF 23-12-1985

##### Article 116

The penalty and the security measure are extinguished, with all their effects, by fulfillment of those or of the sanctions by which they have been substituted or commuted. Likewise, the sanction that has been suspended shall be extinguished by fulfillment of the requirements established upon granting it, in the terms and within the legally applicable periods.  
Article amended DOF 23-12-1985

#### CHAPTER VIII

Suppression of the Criminal Type
Chapter added DOF 23-12-1985. Denomination amended DOF 17-06-2016

##### Article 117

The law that suppresses the criminal type or modifies it, extinguishes, as the case may be, the criminal action or the corresponding sanction, in accordance with the provisions of article 56.  

Article amended DOF 23-12-1985

#### CHAPTER IX

Existence of a Prior Judgment Issued in Proceedings Followed for the Same Facts
Chapter added DOF 23-12-1985

##### Article 118

No one may be tried twice for the same offense, whether acquitted or convicted in the trial. When a sentence has been issued in a proceeding and it appears that another exists in relation to the same person and for the same facts considered in the former, the second proceeding shall conclude by means of a resolution to be issued ex officio by the authority hearing the case. If two sentences exist regarding the same facts, the effects of the one issued second shall be extinguished.  
Errata to the article DOF 31-08-1931. Amended DOF 23-12-1985

#### CHAPTER X

Extinction of Treatment Measures for Persons Not Criminally Liable
Chapter added DOF 23-12-1985

##### Article 118

Bis.- When the person not criminally liable subject to a treatment measure is a fugitive and is subsequently detained, the execution of the treatment measure shall be considered extinguished if it is proven that the personal conditions of the subject no longer correspond to those that would have given rise to its imposition.  
Article added DOF 13-01-1984

TITLE SIX
Concerning minors

##### Article 119.- (Repealed)

Errata to the article DOF 31-08-1931. Partially repealed DOF 02-08-1974. Repealed DOF 24-12-1991

##### Article 120.- (Repealed)

Article partially repealed DOF 02-08-1974. Repealed DOF 24-12-1991

##### Article 121.- (Repealed)

Article partially repealed DOF 02-08-1974. Repealed DOF 24-12-1991

##### Article 122.- (Repealed)

Article partially repealed DOF 02-08-1974. Repealed DOF 24-12-1991

BOOK TWO

### TITLE FIRST

Offenses against the Security of the Nation
Title with denomination amended DOF 29-07-1970

#### CHAPTER I

Treason against the Homeland

##### Article 123

A penalty of imprisonment from five to forty years and a fine of up to fifty thousand pesos shall be imposed on the Mexican who commits treason against the homeland in any of the following forms:  
I.-	Perform acts against the independence, sovereignty or integrity of the Mexican Nation with the purpose of subjecting it to a foreign person, group or government;  

II.-  Take part in acts of hostility against the Nation, through warlike actions under the orders of a foreign State or cooperate with it in any manner that may harm Mexico.  

When nationals serve as troops, a penalty of imprisonment from one to nine years and a fine of up to ten thousand pesos shall be imposed;  
The person who illegally deprives a person of liberty in the national territory to deliver them to the authorities of another country or transfer them outside of Mexico for such purpose shall be considered within the scenario provided in the first paragraph of this section.  
Paragraph added DOF 17-07-1992

III.- Form part of armed groups directed or advised by foreigners; organized within or outside the country, when their purpose is to attack the independence of the Republic, its sovereignty, its freedom or its territorial integrity or to invade the national territory, even when there is no declaration of war;  
IV.-  Willfully destroy or remove the markers that indicate the boundaries of the national territory, or cause them to be confused, provided that this gives rise to a conflict for the Republic, or the latter is in a state of war;  
V.-  Recruit people to make war against Mexico, with the aid or under the protection of a foreign government;  
VI.- Have, in times of peace or war, relations or intelligence with a foreign person, group or government or give them instructions, information or advice, with the purpose of guiding a possible invasion of the national territory or altering internal peace;  
VII.- Willfully and without authorization provide, in times of peace or war, to a foreign person, group or government, documents, instructions or data on establishments or possible military activities;  
VIII.- Conceal or assist a person who commits acts of espionage, knowing that they perform them;  

IX.- Provide to a foreign State or to armed groups directed by foreigners, the human or material elements to invade the national territory, or facilitate their entry to military posts or deliver or cause to be delivered to them combat units or food or war stores or prevent Mexican troops from receiving such aid;  

X.-  Request the intervention or establishment of a protectorate of a foreign State or request that it make war against Mexico; if what is requested is not carried out, the imprisonment shall be from four to eight years and a fine of up to ten thousand pesos;  
XI.- Invite individuals from another State to take up arms against Mexico or invade the national territory, whatever the motive taken; if any of these acts is not carried out, a penalty of four to eight years of imprisonment and a fine of up to ten thousand pesos shall be applied;  
XII.-	Attempt to alienate or encumber the national territory or contribute to its dismemberment;  

XIII.- Receive any benefit, or accept a promise to receive it, with the purpose of carrying out any of the acts indicated in this article;  

XIV.- Accept from the invader an employment, position or commission and issue, agree to or vote on measures aimed at affirming the intrusive government and weakening the national one; and
XV.- Commit, once war is declared or hostilities are broken, sedition, mutiny, rebellion, terrorism, sabotage or conspiracy.  
Article amended DOF 29-07-1970

##### Article 124

A penalty of imprisonment from five to twenty years and a fine of up to twenty-five thousand pesos shall be applied to the Mexican who:  
I.- Without complying with constitutional provisions, celebrates or executes treaties or pacts of offensive alliance with any State, that produce or may produce war between Mexico and another, or admits foreign troops or war units into the country;  
II.-  In case of a foreign invasion, contributes to the establishment of a de facto government in the places occupied by the enemy, whether by giving their vote, attending meetings, signing minutes or representations or by any other means;  
III.- Accepts from the invader an employment, position or commission, or who, in the occupied place, having obtained it legitimately performs it in favor of the invader; and
IV.- With acts not authorized or approved by the government, provokes a foreign war with Mexico, or exposes Mexicans to suffer vexations or reprisals because of this.  
Article amended DOF 15-01-1951, 05-01-1955, 29-07-1970

##### Article 125

A penalty of two to twelve years of imprisonment and a fine of one thousand to twenty thousand pesos shall be applied to whoever incites the people to recognize the government imposed by the invader or to accept an invasion or foreign protectorate.  
Article amended DOF 15-01-1951, 29-07-1970

##### Article 126

The same penalties shall be applied to foreigners who participate in the commission of the offenses referred to in this Chapter, with the exception of those provided in sections VI and VII of article 123.

#### CHAPTER II

Espionage

Article amended DOF 15-01-1951, 29-07-1970

Chapter relocated DOF 29-07-1970

##### Article 127

A penalty of imprisonment from five to twenty years and a fine of up to fifty thousand pesos shall be applied to the foreigner who in time of peace, with the purpose of guiding a possible invasion of the national territory or altering internal peace, has relations or intelligence with a foreign person, group or government or gives them instructions, information or advice.  
The same penalty shall be imposed on the foreigner who in time of peace provides, without authorization to a foreign person, group or government, documents, instructions, or any data on establishments or possible military activities.  
A penalty of imprisonment from five to forty years and a fine of up to fifty thousand pesos shall be applied to the foreigner who, once war is declared or hostilities are broken against Mexico, has relations or intelligence with the enemy or provides them with information, instructions or documents or any aid that in any way harms or may harm the Mexican Nation.  
Errata to the article DOF 31-08-1931. Amended DOF 15-01-1951, 29-07-1970

##### Article 128

A penalty of five to twenty years imprisonment and a fine of up to fifty thousand pesos shall be imposed on a Mexican who, having in their possession confidential documents or information from a foreign government, reveals them to another government, if this harms the Mexican Nation.  
Article amended DOF 29-07-1970

##### Article 129

A penalty of six months to five years imprisonment and a fine of up to five thousand pesos shall be imposed on anyone who, having knowledge of the activities of a spy and their identity, does not report it to the authorities.  
Article amended DOF 14-11-1941, 15-01-1951, 05-01-1955, 29-07-1970

#### CHAPTER III

Sedition
Chapter relocated with amended denomination DOF 29-07-1970

##### Article 130

A penalty of six months to eight years imprisonment and a fine of up to ten thousand pesos shall be imposed on those who, in a tumultuous manner without the use of weapons, resist or attack the authority to prevent the free exercise of its functions with any of the purposes referred to in Article 132.  
Those who direct, organize, incite, compel or financially sponsor others to commit the crime of sedition shall be subject to a penalty of five to fifteen years imprisonment and a fine of up to twenty thousand pesos.  
Errata to the article DOF 31-08-1931. Amended DOF 15-01-1951, 29-07-1970

#### CHAPTER IV

Riot
Chapter added DOF 29-07-1970

##### Article 131

A penalty of six months to seven years imprisonment and a fine of up to five thousand pesos shall be imposed on those who, in order to exercise a right or pretending its exercise or to prevent compliance with a law, gather tumultuously and disturb public order with the use of violence against persons or property, or threaten the authority to intimidate it or force it to make a determination.  

Those who direct, organize, incite, compel or financially sponsor others to commit the crime of riot shall be subject to a penalty of two to ten years imprisonment and a fine of up to fifteen thousand pesos.  
Article amended DOF 29-07-1970

#### CHAPTER V

Rebellion
Chapter added DOF 29-07-1970

##### Article 132

A penalty of two to twenty years imprisonment and a fine of five thousand to fifty thousand pesos shall be imposed on those who, not being military personnel in active service, with violence and use of weapons attempt to:  
I.-	Abolish or reform the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States;  
II.- Reform, destroy or prevent the integration of the constitutional institutions of the Federation, or their free exercise; and
III.- Remove or prevent the performance of their office by any of the high officials of the Federation mentioned in Article 2 of the Law of Responsibilities of Officials and Employees of the Federation, the Federal District and the High Officials of the States.  
Subsection amended DOF 23-12-1974 Article amended DOF 15-01-1951, 29-07-1970

##### Article 133

The penalties indicated in the preceding article shall be imposed on anyone who, residing in territory occupied by the Federal Government, and without physical or moral coercion, provides the rebels with weapons, ammunition, money, food, means of transportation or communication or prevents the Government troops from receiving such aid. If residing in territory occupied by the rebels, the imprisonment shall be six months to five years.  

A public official or employee of the Federal or State Governments, or of the Municipalities, of decentralized public bodies, of state-owned enterprises, or of public services, federal or local, who having by reason of their office documents or information of strategic interest, provides them to the rebels, shall be subject to a penalty of five to forty years imprisonment and a fine of five thousand to fifty thousand pesos.  
Errata to the article DOF 31-08-1931. Amended DOF 29-07-1970

##### Article 134

A penalty of two to twenty years imprisonment and a fine of five thousand to fifty thousand pesos shall be imposed on those who, not being military personnel in active service, with violence and use of weapons, attack the Government of any of the States of the Federation, against its constitutional institutions or to achieve the removal from office of any of the high officials of the State, when the Powers of the Union intervening in the manner prescribed by Article 122 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, the rebels do not lay down their arms.  
Article amended DOF 15-01-1951, 29-07-1970

##### Article 135

A penalty of one to twenty years imprisonment and a fine of up to fifty thousand pesos shall be imposed on anyone who:  
I.-	In any form or by any means invites to a rebellion;  

II.-	Residing in territory occupied by the Government:
a)	Hides or assists the spies or scouts of the rebels, knowing them to be such;  

b)	Maintains relations with the rebels, to provide them with news concerning military operations or other information useful to them.  
III.-	Voluntarily serves an employment, position or commission in a place occupied by the rebels, unless acting under coercion or for humanitarian reasons.  
Article amended DOF 15-01-1951, 29-07-1970

##### Article 136

Government officials or agents and rebels who, after combat, directly or through orders, cause the death of prisoners, shall be subject to a penalty of fifteen to thirty years imprisonment and a fine of ten thousand to twenty thousand pesos.  
Article amended DOF 29-07-1970

##### Article 137

When during a rebellion the crimes of homicide, robbery, kidnapping, dispossession, arson, looting or other crimes are committed, the rules of concurrence shall apply.  
Rebels shall not be responsible for homicides or injuries inflicted in the act of combat, but for those caused outside of it, both the one who commands them and the one who permits them and those who immediately execute them shall be responsible.  
Article amended DOF 15-01-1951, 29-07-1970

##### Article 138

No penalty shall be imposed on those who lay down their arms before being taken prisoner, if they have not committed any of the crimes mentioned in the preceding article.  
Article amended DOF 29-07-1970

#### CHAPTER VI

Terrorism
Chapter added DOF 29-07-1970

##### Article 139

A penalty of fifteen to forty years imprisonment and four hundred to one thousand two hundred days fine shall be imposed, without prejudice to the penalties corresponding to other resulting crimes:  
I.	On anyone who, using toxic substances, chemical, biological or similar weapons, radioactive material, nuclear material, nuclear fuel, radioactive mineral, radiation source or instruments that emit radiation, explosives, or firearms, or by fire, flood or by any other violent means, intentionally carries out acts against property or services, whether public or private, or against the physical or emotional integrity, or the life of persons, that produce alarm, fear or terror in the population or in a group or sector thereof, to attack national security or pressure the authority or a private individual, or force the latter to make a determination.  

II.	On anyone who agrees to or prepares a terrorist act that is intended to be committed, is being committed or has been committed in national territory.  
The sanctions referred to in the first paragraph of this article shall be increased by one half, when in addition:  
I.	The crime is committed against publicly accessible real property;  

II.	Damage or harm is generated to the national economy, or
III.	In the commission of the crime a person is detained as a hostage.  
Article amended DOF 29-07-1970, 28-06-2007, 14-03-2014

##### Article 139

Bis.- A penalty of one to nine years imprisonment and one hundred to three hundred days fine shall be imposed on anyone who harbors a terrorist, having knowledge of their activities or identity.  
Article added DOF 28-06-2007

##### Article 139

Ter.- A penalty of five to fifteen years of imprisonment and two hundred to six hundred days' fine shall be imposed on whoever threatens to commit the crime of terrorism referred to in the first paragraph of Article 139.  
Article added DOF 28-06-2007

#### CHAPTER VI BIS

Financing of Terrorism
Chapter added DOF 14-03-2014

##### Article 139

Quáter.- The same penalty specified in Article 139 of this Code shall be imposed, without prejudice to the penalties corresponding to other resulting crimes, on whoever by any means whatsoever, whether directly or indirectly, contributes or collects economic funds or resources of any nature, with knowledge that they will be destined to finance or support activities of terrorist individuals or organizations, or to be used, or intended to be used, directly or indirectly, totally or partially, for the commission, in national territory or abroad, of any of the crimes provided for in the following legal provisions:  
I.	From the Federal Penal Code, the following:

1)	Terrorism, provided for in Articles 139, 139 Bis and 139 Ter;  

2)	Sabotage, provided for in Article 140;  
3)	International Terrorism, provided for in Articles 148 Bis, 148 Ter and 148 Quáter;  

4)	Attacks on communication routes, provided for in Articles 167, section IX, and 170, first, second and third paragraphs, and
5)	Robbery, provided for in Article 368 Quinquies.  
II.	From the Law Declaring as Mining Reserves the Deposits of Uranium, Thorium and other Substances from which Fissile Isotopes that can produce Nuclear Energy are obtained, those provided for in Articles 10 and 13.  
Article added DOF 14-03-2014

##### Article 139

Quinquies.- One to nine years of imprisonment and one hundred to three hundred days' fine shall be imposed on whoever conceals a person who has participated in the crimes provided for in Article 139 Quáter of this Code.

#### CHAPTER VII

Sabotage

Article added DOF 14-03-2014

Chapter added DOF 29-07-1970

##### Article 140

A penalty of two to twenty years of imprisonment and a fine of one thousand to fifty thousand pesos shall be imposed on whoever damages, destroys, harms or unlawfully obstructs communication routes, public services, functions of State agencies, decentralized public bodies, state-owned enterprises or their facilities; steel, electric or basic industry plants; production or distribution centers for necessary consumer goods, weapons, ammunition or war implements, with the purpose of disrupting the economic life of the country or affecting its defense capacity.  
Paragraph reformed DOF 14-07-2014

A penalty of six months to five years of imprisonment and a fine of up to five thousand pesos shall be imposed on whoever, having knowledge of the activities of a saboteur and of his identity, does not report it to the authorities.  

The penalties referred to in the first paragraph of this article shall be increased by up to one half, when acts of sabotage are carried out on the pipelines, equipment, facilities or assets of assignees, contractors, permit holders or distributors referred to in the Federal Law to Prevent and Punish Crimes Committed in Matters of Hydrocarbons.

#### CHAPTER VIII

Conspiracy

Paragraph added DOF 12-01-2016 Article reformed DOF 29-07-1970

Chapter added DOF 29-07-1970

##### Article 141

A penalty of one to nine years of imprisonment and a fine of up to ten thousand pesos shall be imposed on those who jointly resolve to commit one or more of the crimes of this Title and agree on the means to carry out their determination.  
Article reformed DOF 29-07-1970

#### CHAPTER IX

Common provisions for the chapters of this Title
Chapter added DOF 29-07-1970

##### Article 142

Whoever instigates, incites or invites the execution of the crimes provided for in this Title shall be subject to the same penalty specified for the crime in question, with the exception of what is established in the second paragraph of Article 130, in the second paragraph of Article 131 and in section I of Article 135, which retain their specific penalty.  
Whoever instigates, incites or invites military personnel in service, to the execution of the crimes referred to in this Title, shall be subject to a penalty of five to forty years of imprisonment, with the exception of the crime of terrorism, whose penalty shall be eight to forty years of imprisonment and five hundred to one thousand one hundred fifty days' fine.  
Paragraph reformed DOF 28-06-2007 Article reformed DOF 15-01-1951, 29-07-1970

##### Article 143

When other crimes result from the commission of the crimes referred to in this Title, the rules of concurrence shall apply.  
In addition to the penalties specified in this Title, those responsible, if they are Mexican, shall be subject to suspension of their political rights for a period of up to ten years, which shall be computed from the completion of their sentence. In the crimes included in Chapters I and II of this Title, suspension of such rights shall be imposed for up to forty years.  
Erratum to article DOF 31-08-1931. Reformed DOF 29-07-1970

##### Article 144

The crimes of rebellion, sedition, mutiny and conspiracy to commit them shall be considered crimes of a political nature.  
Article reformed DOF 15-01-1951, 29-07-1970

##### Article 145

A penalty of five to forty years of imprisonment and one hundred twenty to one thousand one hundred fifty days' fine shall be imposed on the official or employee of the Federal or State Governments, or of the Municipalities, of decentralized public bodies, of state-owned enterprises or of public services, federal or local, or of autonomous constitutional bodies, who incur in any of the crimes provided for by this Title, with the exception of the crime of terrorism, whose penalty shall be nine to forty-five years of imprisonment and five hundred to one thousand one hundred fifty days' fine.  
Erratum to article DOF 31-08-1931. Reformed DOF 14-11-1941, 15-01-1951, 29-07-1970, 28-06-2007, 14-07-2014

##### Article 145-Bis.- (Repealed)

Article added DOF 14-11-1941. Repealed (by comprehensive reform of Title One of Book Two) DOF 29-07-1970

TITLE TWO
CRIMES AGAINST INTERNATIONAL LAW
Title renumbered (formerly Title Three) DOF 29-07-1970

#### CHAPTER I

Piracy

##### Article 146.- The following shall be considered pirates

I.-  Those who, belonging to the crew of a Mexican merchant vessel, of another nation, or without nationality, seize by armed force any vessel, or commit depredations on it, or commit violence against persons on board;  
II.- Those who, being on board a vessel, seize it and voluntarily deliver it to a pirate, and
III.-	Repealed.  
Section repealed DOF 21-06-2018

##### Article 147

Fifteen to thirty years of imprisonment and confiscation of the vessel shall be imposed on those who belong to a pirate crew.

#### CHAPTER II

Violation of immunity and neutrality

##### Article 148

Imprisonment of three days to two years and a fine of one hundred to two thousand pesos shall be imposed for:

I.-	The violation of any diplomatic immunity, real or personal, of a foreign sovereign, or of the representative of another nation, whether they reside in the Republic or are passing through it;  
II.-	The violation of the duties of neutrality that correspond to the Mexican nation, when done knowingly;  
III.-	The violation of the immunity of a parliamentarian, or that granted by a safe-conduct, and
IV.-	Any attack or violence of any kind against the shields, emblems or flags of another foreign Nation.  
Section reformed DOF 06-01-2023

In the case of section III, and if circumstances warrant it, judges may impose up to six years of imprisonment.

#### CHAPTER III INTERNATIONAL TERRORISM

Chapter added DOF 28-06-2007

##### Artículo 148

Bis.- A penalty of fifteen to forty years of imprisonment and four hundred to one thousand two hundred days' fine shall be imposed, without prejudice to the penalties corresponding to other resulting crimes:  
I.	On whoever, using toxic substances, chemical, biological or similar weapons, radioactive material, nuclear material, nuclear fuel, radioactive mineral, radiation source or instruments that emit radiation, explosives or firearms, or by fire, flood or any other violent means, carries out acts in Mexican territory against property, persons or services of a foreign State, or of any international body or organization, that produce alarm, fear or terror in the population or in a group or sector thereof, in order to pressure the authority of that foreign State, or compel it or an international body or organization to take a particular decision;  

II.	On whoever commits the crime of homicide or any act against the liberty of an internationally protected person;  
III.	On whoever carries out, in Mexican territory, any violent act against official premises, private residences or means of transport of an internationally protected person, that threatens their life or liberty, or
IV.	On whoever agrees to or prepares in Mexican territory a terrorist act that is intended to be committed, is being committed or has been committed abroad.  

For the purposes of this article, an internationally protected person shall be understood as a head of State including each of the members of a collegiate body when, in accordance with the respective constitution, they perform the functions of head of State, a head of government or a minister of foreign affairs, as well as members of their family who accompany them and, in addition, any representative, official or official personality of a State or any official, official personality or other agent of an intergovernmental organization who, at the time and place where a crime is committed against them, the members of their family who reside with them, their official premises, their private residence or their means of transport, is entitled to special protection under international law.  
Artículo adicionado DOF 28-06-2007. Reformado DOF 14-03-2014

##### Artículo 148

Ter.- A penalty of five to ten years of imprisonment and one hundred to three hundred days' fine shall be imposed on whoever harbors a terrorist, having knowledge of their identity or that they carry out any of the activities provided for in this chapter.  
Artículo adicionado DOF 28-06-2007

##### Artículo 148

Quáter.- A penalty of six to twelve years of imprisonment and two hundred to six hundred days' fine shall be imposed on whoever threatens to commit the crime of terrorism referred to in sections I to III of article 148 Bis.  
Artículo adicionado DOF 28-06-2007. Reformado DOF 14-03-2014

TITULO TERCERO
Crimes against humanity
Título adicionado DOF 20-01-1967. Recorrido (antes Título Cuarto) DOF 29-07-1970

#### CAPITULO I

Violation of the duties of humanity
Capítulo adicionado DOF 20-01-1967

##### Artículo 149

Whoever violates the duties of humanity toward prisoners and hostages of war, the wounded, or in field hospitals, shall be subject for that fact alone to: imprisonment of three to six years, except as provided, for special cases, in military laws.

#### CAPITULO II

Genocide
Capítulo adicionado DOF 20-01-1967

##### Artículo 149

Bis.- Whoever commits the crime of genocide with the purpose of destroying, totally or partially, one or more national groups or groups of an ethnic, racial or religious character, perpetrating by any means crimes against the life of members of those groups, or imposing mass sterilization with the aim of preventing the reproduction of the group.  

For such crime, twenty to forty years of imprisonment and a fine of fifteen thousand to twenty thousand pesos shall be imposed.  
If with identical purpose attacks are carried out on the bodily integrity or health of the members of said communities or minors under eighteen years of age are transferred from them to other groups, using physical or moral violence for this purpose, the penalty shall be five to twenty years of imprisonment and a fine of two thousand to seven thousand pesos.  
Párrafo reformado DOF 09-03-2018

The same penalties indicated in the preceding paragraph shall be applied to whoever with the same purpose intentionally subjects the group to conditions of existence that are to bring about its physical destruction, total or partial.  

In the event that those responsible for said crimes are rulers, officials or public employees and commit them in the exercise of their functions or by reason thereof, in addition to the penalties established in this article, the penalties indicated in article 15 of the Law on Responsibilities of Officials and Employees of the Federation shall be applied to them.  

Título Tercero Bis
Crimes against the Dignity of Persons

Capítulo Único Discrimination

Artículo adicionado DOF 20-01-1967

Título adicionado DOF 14-06-2012

Capítulo adicionado DOF 14-06-2012

##### Artículo 149

Ter. A penalty of one to three years of imprisonment or one hundred fifty to three hundred days of work for the benefit of the community and up to two hundred days' fine shall be imposed on whoever, for reasons of ethnic or national origin or belonging, race, skin color, language, gender, sex, sexual preference, age, marital status, national or social origin, social or economic condition, health condition, pregnancy, political opinions or of any other nature, attacks human dignity or annuls or diminishes the rights and freedoms of persons by carrying out any of the following conducts:

I.	Denies a person a service or benefit to which they are entitled;  
II.	Denies or restricts labor rights, primarily by reason of gender or pregnancy; or limits a health service, primarily to women in relation to pregnancy; or
III.	Denies or restricts educational rights.  

The public servant who, for the reasons provided in the first paragraph of this article, denies or delays to a person a procedure, service or benefit to which they are entitled shall have the penalty provided in the first paragraph of this article increased by one half, and in addition shall be subject to dismissal and disqualification from holding any public office, employment or commission, for the same period as the deprivation of liberty imposed.  

All those measures tending to the protection of socially disadvantaged groups shall not be considered discriminatory.  

When the conducts referred to in this article are committed by a person with whom the victim has a relationship of labor subordination, the penalty shall be increased by one half.  

Likewise, the penalty shall be increased when discriminatory acts limit access to the legal guarantees indispensable for the protection of all human rights.  

This crime shall be prosecuted upon complaint.  

TITULO CUARTO

Artículo adicionado DOF 14-06-2012

Crimes against public security
Título recorrido DOF 20-01-1967. Recorrido (antes Título Quinto) DOF 29-07-1970

#### CAPITULO I

Escape of prisoners

##### Artículo 150

Six months to nine years of imprisonment shall be imposed on whoever facilitates the escape of any detainee, accused or convicted person. If the detainee or accused person is charged with a crime or crimes against health, the person who facilitates their escape shall be subject to seven to fifteen years of imprisonment, or, in the case of the escape of a convicted person, the penalty shall be increased up to twenty years of imprisonment.  

If the person who facilitates the escape is a public servant, the penalty shall be increased by one third of the penalties indicated in this article, as applicable. In addition, they shall be dismissed from their employment and disqualified from obtaining another for a period of eight to twelve years.  
Artículo reformado DOF 17-11-1986

##### Article 151.- Repealed

Article repealed DOF 27-03-2017

##### Article 152

Whoever facilitates at the same time, or in a single act, the evasion of several persons deprived of liberty by the competent authority, shall be imposed up to one-half more of the custodial sanctions indicated in article 150, as applicable.  
Article amended DOF 17-11-1986

##### Article 153

If the recapture of the fugitive is achieved through efforts of the person responsible for the evasion, ten to one hundred eighty days of community service shall be applied to the latter, according to the seriousness of the crime imputed to the prisoner or detainee, except as provided by article 150, second paragraph.  
Article amended DOF 10-01-1994, 27-03-2017

##### Article 154

A person deprived of liberty who escapes shall be imposed six months to three years of imprisonment; this penalty shall be increased by one-third when the person acts in concert with one or more other persons deprived of liberty and any of them escapes or exercises violence against persons.  
Article amended DOF 27-03-2017

#### CHAPTER II

Breach of sanction

##### Article 155

The convict who escapes while under any custodial sanction, or in detention or preventive imprisonment, shall not be credited the time spent outside the place where it must be served, nor shall the good conduct had before the escape be taken into account.

##### Article 156.- (Repealed)

Article repealed DOF 25-05-2011

##### Article 157

The person sentenced to confinement who leaves the place fixed for residence before completing it shall be subject to imprisonment for the time remaining to complete the confinement.

##### Article 158

Fifteen to ninety days of community service shall be imposed:
Paragraph amended DOF 10-01-1994

I.-	On the convict subject to police surveillance who does not provide the police with the information requested about conduct, and
II.-	On the person who has been prohibited from going to or residing in a certain place, if the prohibition is violated.  
If the sentenced person was convicted for a serious crime so classified by law, the aforementioned sanction shall be one to four years of imprisonment.  
Paragraph added DOF 13-05-1996

##### Article 159

The convict suspended from profession or trade, or disqualified from exercising them, who breaches the sentence, shall pay a fine of twenty to one thousand pesos. In case of recidivism, the fine shall be doubled and imprisonment of one to six years shall be applied.  
Errata to the article DOF 31-08-1931

#### CHAPTER III

Prohibited weapons

##### Article 160

Whoever carries, manufactures, imports, sells or stockpiles without a lawful purpose or with the intention to attack, instruments that can be used for attack or defense, shall be imposed imprisonment of one to six years and five hundred to one thousand times the daily value of the Unit of Measurement and Update, as well as confiscation.  
Paragraph amended DOF 30-12-1991, 19-02-2021

Public servants may carry the weapons necessary for the exercise of their office, subject to the regulations of the respective laws.  
These crimes, whose jurisdiction belongs to the common jurisdiction, shall be sanctioned without prejudice to the provisions of the Federal Law on Firearms and Explosives, of federal application with regard to these objects.  
Article amended DOF 15-01-1951, 13-01-1984

##### Article 161

A special license is required for carrying or selling pistols or revolvers.

##### Article 162

Six months to three years of imprisonment or 180 to 360 days fine and confiscation shall be applied:
Paragraph amended DOF 15-01-1951, 30-12-1991
I.-	Repealed.  
Section repealed DOF 19-02-2021

II.-	To whoever offers for sale pistols or revolvers, lacking the necessary permit;  
III.-	Repealed.  
Section repealed DOF 19-02-2021

IV.-	To whoever, without a lawful purpose or without the corresponding permit, stockpiles weapons, and

V.-	To whoever, without a license, carries any of the weapons indicated in article 161.  

In all cases included in this article, in addition to the indicated sanctions, the weapons shall be confiscated.  
Officials and agents of authority may carry the weapons necessary for the exercise of their office.  
Repealing provision DOF 10-11-1939. Article reinstated by provision of validity DOF 27-12-1941

##### Article 163

The granting of licenses referred to in article 161 shall be made by the Executive of the Union through the Department or Secretariat it designates, subject to the provisions of the respective regulatory law, and to the following:  
I.-	The sale of weapons included in article 161 may only be made by commercial establishments provided with a license and never by individuals, and
II.-	Whoever requests a license to carry weapons must comply with the following requirements:
Errata to the paragraph DOF 31-08-1931

a).	Post a bond for the amount set by the authority, and

b).	Prove the need to carry weapons and background of honorability and prudence, with the testimony of five persons well known to the authority.

#### CHAPTER IV

Criminal associations

##### Article 164

Whoever forms part of an association or band of three or more persons with the purpose of committing crimes shall be imposed imprisonment of five to ten years and one hundred to three hundred days fine.  
Paragraph amended DOF 08-02-1999

When the member of the association is or has been a public servant of any police corporation, the penalty referred to in the preceding paragraph shall be increased by one-half and there shall be imposed, in addition, dismissal from public employment, office or commission and disqualification of one to five years to hold another. If the member of the association belongs to the Mexican Armed Forces in retirement, reserve or active status, likewise the penalty shall be increased by one-half and the definitive discharge from the Armed Force to which the person belongs shall be imposed and the person shall be disqualified for one to five years to hold public office or commission.  
Paragraph amended DOF 10-01-1994 Errata to the article DOF 31-08-1931. Amended DOF 03-01-1989

##### Article 164

Bis.- When any crime is committed by a gang, up to one-half more of the penalties corresponding to them for the crime or crimes committed shall be applied to those who participate in its commission.  
For the purposes of this provision, a gang is understood to be the habitual, occasional or transitory gathering of three or more persons who, without being organized for criminal purposes, commit a crime in common.  

When the member of the gang is or has been a public servant of any police corporation, the penalty shall be increased up to two-thirds of the penalties corresponding for the crime or crimes committed and there shall be imposed in addition, dismissal from public employment, office or commission and disqualification of one to five years to hold another.  
Article added DOF 08-03-1968. Amended DOF 03-01-1989

TITLE FIVE
Crimes regarding means of communication and correspondence
Errata to the denomination DOF 31-08-1931. Denomination amended DOF 15-01-1951. Title renumbered DOF 20-01-1967. Renumbered
(formerly Title Six) DOF 29-07-1970

#### CHAPTER I

Attacks on means of communication and violation of correspondence
Errata to the denomination of the Chapter DOF 31-08-1931. Denomination amended DOF 15-01-1951

##### Article 165

Public roads are defined as transit routes habitually intended for public use, whoever the owner may be, and whatever the means of locomotion permitted and the dimensions it may have; excluding the sections that are within the limits of populated areas.

##### Article 166

Whoever removes, cuts or destroys the moorings that hold a vessel or other vehicle, or removes the obstacle that prevents or moderates its movement, shall be subject to imprisonment of fifteen days to two years if no damage results; if damage is caused, the corresponding sanction for the resulting crime shall also be applied.  
Article amended DOF 15-01-1951

##### Article 166

Bis.- Persons who by reason of their position or employment in telecommunications companies unlawfully provide information about persons who make use of those means of communication shall be subject to a penalty of three months to three years imprisonment and shall be removed from their position.  

In the preceding case, the penalty shall be increased by up to one half when the crime is committed by a public servant in the exercise of their functions. Likewise, in addition to the penalties indicated, removal from employment shall be imposed and they shall be disqualified for one to ten years from holding public office or commission.  
Article added DOF 14-07-2014

##### Article 167

Imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of one hundred to ten thousand days shall be imposed:
Paragraph amended DOF 15-01-1951. Errata DOF 31-05-1956. Amended DOF 11-01-1982, 17-05-1999

I.-  For the sole act of removing or modifying without proper authorization: one or more sleepers, rails, spikes, screws, plates and other similar objects that secure them, or a railway switch of a public use railroad;  
II.- Whoever destroys or separates one or more posts, insulators, wires, machines or apparatus employed in the telegraph service; any of the components of the telecommunications network employed in the telephone, switching or radiocommunication service, or any component of an installation for the production of magnetic or electromagnetic energy or its means of transmission;  
Section amended DOF 17-05-1999, 14-07-2014

III.-  Whoever, in order to stop vehicles on a public road, or prevent the passage of a locomotive, or derail it or the wagons, removes or destroys the objects mentioned in section I, places any hindrance, or any suitable obstacle;  
IV.-  For the burning of a wagon, or of any other vehicle intended for the transport of cargo and which is not part of a train in which any person is present;  
V.-  Whoever floods in whole or in part a public road or pours water onto it in a manner that causes damage;  

VI.- Whoever intentionally or for profit interrupts or interferes with wired, wireless or fiber optic communications, whether telegraphic, telephonic or satellite, by means of which audio, video or data signals are transferred;  
Section amended DOF 17-05-1999

VII.	Whoever destroys in whole or in part, or paralyzes by other means specified in the preceding sections, a machine employed on a railroad, or a vessel, or destroys or damages a bridge, a dike, a causeway or road, or a track;  
Section amended DOF 28-06-2007

VIII.	Whoever with the purpose of harming or hindering communications, modifies or alters the mechanism of a vehicle causing it to lose power, speed or safety, and
Section added DOF 15-01-1951. Amended DOF 28-06-2007

IX.	Whoever disseminates or transmits false information that in any way harms or may harm the safety of an aircraft, a ship or another type of federal public service vehicle.  
Section added DOF 11-01-1982. Repealed DOF 07-11-1996. Added DOF 28-06-2007

##### Article 168

Whoever, for the execution of the acts referred to in the preceding articles, uses explosives, shall be subject to imprisonment of fifteen to twenty years.

##### Article 168

bis.- Imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of three hundred to three thousand days shall be imposed on whoever without right:  
I.	Deciphers or decodes telecommunications signals other than satellite signals carrying programs;  
Section amended DOF 01-07-2020

II.	Transfers the ownership, use or enjoyment of apparatus, instruments or information that permit the deciphering or decoding of telecommunications signals other than satellite signals carrying programs, or
Section amended DOF 01-07-2020

III.	Receives or distributes an encrypted satellite signal carrying programs originally encoded, without the authorization of the legal distributor of the signal.  
Section added DOF 01-07-2020 Article added DOF 17-05-1999

##### Article 168

ter. A penalty of twelve to fifteen years imprisonment shall be imposed on whoever manufactures, commercializes, acquires, installs, carries, uses or operates equipment that blocks, cancels or nullifies cellular telephone, radiocommunication or data or image transmission signals with the exception of what is established in the second paragraph of article 190 Bis of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law.  

The equipment referred to in the first paragraph of article 190 Bis of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law shall be seized in terms of what is established in the National Code of Criminal Procedures and must subsequently be destroyed in its entirety.  
If the crime referred to in the first paragraph of this article were committed by public servants, and without express written authorization duly accredited by their immediate superior, a penalty of fifteen to eighteen years imprisonment shall be imposed.  
Article added DOF 24-01-2020

##### Article 169

Whoever sets in motion a locomotive, car, truck or similar vehicle and abandons it or, in any other way, makes control of its speed impossible and may cause damage, shall be subject to imprisonment of one to six years.

##### Article 170

Whoever by employing explosives or incendiary materials, or by any other means totally or partially destroys installations or services of air or maritime navigation or of airports that provide services to civil aviation, any fixed platform, or a vessel, aircraft, or other vehicle of federal or local public service, or that provides services to the public, if they are occupied by one or more persons, shall be subject to twenty to thirty years imprisonment.  

If no person is present in the vehicle, installation or platform in question, imprisonment of five to twenty years shall be applied.  

Likewise, imprisonment of three to twenty years and a fine of one hundred to four hundred days shall be imposed, without prejudice to the penalty corresponding to other crimes committed, on whoever through violence, threats or deceit, seizes or exercises control of a fixed platform, installations or services of air or maritime navigation or of airports that provide services to civil aviation; as well as of a vessel, aircraft, railway machine or train, buses or any other means of collective, interstate or international public transport, or causes them to deviate from their route or destination.  
When any of the crimes contemplated in this article and in 168 are committed by a public servant of any police corporation, in addition to the penalties indicated in these articles, removal from employment shall be imposed and they shall be disqualified for one to ten years from holding public office or commission. If whoever commits the mentioned crimes is a member of the Mexican Armed Forces in retirement, reserve or active status, they shall also be subject to definitive discharge from the Armed Force to which they belong and shall be disqualified for one to ten years from holding public office or commission.  

For purposes of this article, fixed platform shall be understood as an artificial island, installation or structure permanently attached to the seabed or continental shelf for purposes of exploration or exploitation of resources or other purposes of an economic nature.  
Article amended DOF 05-01-1955, 24-12-1968, 10-01-1994, 14-03-2014

##### Article 171

Imprisonment of up to six months, a fine of up to one hundred pesos and suspension or loss of the right to use a driver's license shall be imposed:  
I.-	(Repealed)
Section repealed DOF 30-12-1991

II.-  On whoever in a state of intoxication or under the influence of narcotic drugs commits any infraction of traffic and circulation regulations while driving motor vehicles, independently of the sanction that corresponds if damage is caused to persons or property.  
Article amended DOF 15-01-1951

##### Article 172

When any damage is caused by means of any vehicle, motor or machinery, in addition to applying the sanctions for the resulting crime, the offender shall be disqualified from operating such apparatus for a time not less than one month nor exceeding one year. In case of recidivism, the disqualification shall be definitive.

#### CHAPTER I BIS

Illicit use of installations intended for air traffic.  
Chapter added DOF 10-01-1986

##### Article 172

Bis.- Whoever for the performance of criminal activities uses or permits the use of aerodromes, airports, heliports, landing strips or any other installation intended for air traffic that are his property or under his charge and care, shall be imposed imprisonment of two to six years and from one hundred to three hundred days' fine and confiscation of the instruments, objects or proceeds of the crime, whatever their nature. If said installations are clandestine, the penalty shall be increased by up to one half.  

The same penalties shall be imposed on those who conduct clandestine flights, or provide the means to facilitate the landing or takeoff of aircraft or provide refueling or maintenance to aircraft used in such activities.  

If the criminal activities referred to in the first paragraph are related to crimes against health, the penalties of imprisonment and fine shall be doubled.  
Whoever constructs, installs, conditions or puts into operation the real property and installations referred to in the first paragraph, without having observed the concession, notice or permit standards contained in the respective legislation, shall be imposed from three to eight years of imprisonment and from one hundred fifty to four hundred days' fine.  

The sanctions provided in this article shall be imposed without prejudice to the measures provided by the General Law of Communication Routes and the sanctions that correspond, as the case may be, for other crimes committed.  
Article added DOF 10-01-1986. Amended DOF 10-01-1994

#### CHAPTER II

Violation of correspondence

##### Article 173

From three to one hundred eighty days of community service shall be applied:
Paragraph amended DOF 30-12-1991, 10-01-1994

I.-	To whoever improperly opens a written communication not addressed to him, and

II.-	To whoever improperly intercepts a written communication not addressed to him, even if he keeps it sealed and does not learn of its contents.  
The crimes provided in this article shall be prosecuted upon complaint.  
Paragraph added DOF 30-12-1991

##### Article 174

Parents who open or intercept written communications addressed to their minor children are not considered to act criminally, nor are guardians with respect to persons under their dependence, nor spouses between themselves.

##### Article 175

The provision of article 173 does not include correspondence circulating through the post office, with respect to which the provisions of postal legislation shall be observed.

##### Article 176

An employee of a telegraph, telephone station or wireless station who knowingly fails to transmit a message delivered to him for that purpose, or to communicate to the addressee one received from another office, if he causes damage, shall be imposed from fifteen days to one year of imprisonment or from 30 to 180 days' fine.  
Errata to article DOF 31-08-1931. Amended DOF 30-12-1991

##### Article 177

Whoever intercepts private communications without a mandate from competent judicial authority shall be subject to sanctions of six to twelve years of imprisonment and from three hundred to six hundred days' fine.  
Article repealed DOF 30-12-1991. Added DOF 07-11-1996

TITLE SIXTH
Crimes Against Authority
Title renumbered DOF 20-01-1967. Renumbered (formerly Title Seventh) DOF 29-07-1970

#### CHAPTER I

Disobedience and resistance of private individuals

##### Article 178

Whoever, without legitimate cause, refuses to provide a service of public interest to which the Law obliges him, or disobeys a legitimate mandate of authority, shall be subject to from fifteen to two hundred days of community service.  
Whoever disobeys the mandate of house arrest or the prohibition to leave a geographic demarcation, issued by competent judicial authority, shall be imposed from six months to two years of imprisonment and from ten to two hundred days' fine.  
Paragraph added DOF 08-02-1999 Article amended DOF 30-12-1991, 10-01-1994

##### Article 178

Bis.- A natural person or, as the case may be, the representative of a legal person who is required by the Public Prosecutor's Office or by the competent authority to collaborate or provide information for the geographic location, in real time, of communication devices in terms of the provisions of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law, that are related to investigations regarding organized crime, crimes against health, kidnapping, extortion, threats or any of those provided in chapter II of Title Ninth of the Federal Penal Code and who willfully refuses to do so, shall be imposed a penalty of imprisonment from 3 to 8 years and from five thousand to ten thousand days' fine.  

The same penalties shall apply to the natural person, or as the case may be to the representative of the legal person who willfully obstructs, delays without just cause or refuses to collaborate in the interception of private communications, or to provide information to which they are obligated, in the terms of applicable legislation.  
The same penalties shall apply to the natural person, or as the case may be to the representative of the legal person who is required by the competent authorities to collaborate or provide information for the geographic location, in real time, of communication devices in terms of the provisions of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law and who willfully refuses to do so.  
Article added DOF 17-04-2012. Amended DOF 14-07-2014

##### Article 179

Whoever without legal excuse refuses to appear before the authority to give his statement, when legally required, shall not be considered guilty of the crime provided in the preceding article, except when he insists on his disobedience after having been compelled by the judicial authority or warned by the administrative authority, as the case may be, to appear to testify.

##### Article 180

From one to two years of imprisonment and a fine of ten to one thousand pesos shall be applied: to whoever, employing force, threat or menace, opposes the public authority or its agents from exercising any of their functions or resists compliance with a legitimate mandate executed in legal form.  
Errata to article DOF 31-08-1931

##### Article 180

Bis. From one to two years of imprisonment and from ten thousand to thirty thousand days' fine shall be applied to whoever removes, modifies or renders useless, without due authorization, location and surveillance devices.  
If the conduct referred to in the preceding paragraph is carried out by a member of any public security institution, from two to five years of imprisonment, from twenty thousand to forty thousand days' fine and disqualification from exercising any employment or public office in any sphere of government for up to twenty years shall be applied.  
Article added DOF 30-11-2010

##### Article 181

Coercion made to a public authority by means of physical or moral violence, to compel it to execute an official act without the legal requirements or another that is not within its powers, shall be equivalent to resistance and shall be punished with the same penalty as the latter.

##### Article 182

Anyone who, being required to be examined in trial, without the exceptions established by this Code or by the Code of Criminal Procedure being available to them, and after enforcement measures have been exhausted, refuses to take the legal oath or to testify, shall pay a fine of 10 to 30 days. In case of recidivism, imprisonment of one to six months or a fine of 30 to 90 days shall be imposed.  
Article amended DOF 30-12-1991

##### Article 183

When the law authorizes the use of enforcement to make effective the determinations of the authority, the crime of disobedience shall only be consummated when the enforcement measures have been exhausted.

#### CHAPTER II

Opposition to the execution of any public work or works

##### Article 184.- (Repealed)

Article repealed DOF 30-12-1991

##### Article 185

When several persons by common agreement attempt to prevent the execution of a public work or works, or of those intended for the provision of a public service, ordered to be done with the legal requirements by competent authority, or with its authorization, they shall be punished with three months to one year of imprisonment, if only a simple material opposition without violence is made. In case of violence, the penalty shall be up to two years.  
Article amended DOF 30-12-1991, 13-05-1996

##### Article 186.- (Repealed)

Errata to the article DOF 31-08-1931. Repealed DOF 30-12-1991

#### CHAPTER III

Breaking of seals

##### Article 187

Anyone who breaks the seals placed by order of the public authority shall be subject to thirty to one hundred eighty days of community service.  
Article amended DOF 30-12-1991, 10-01-1994

##### Article 188

When the parties interested in a civil matter break by common agreement the seals placed by the public authority, they shall pay a fine of twenty to two hundred pesos.

#### CHAPTER IV

Crimes committed against public officials

##### Article 189

Anyone who commits a crime against a public servant or agent of authority in the act of lawfully exercising their functions or by reason thereof, shall be subject to one to six years of imprisonment, in addition to that which corresponds for the crime committed.  
Errata to the article DOF 31-08-1931. Amended DOF 13-05-1996

##### Article 190.- (Repealed)

Errata to the article DOF 31-08-1931. Repealed DOF 30-12-1991

#### CHAPTER V

Outrages to national symbols

##### Article 191

Anyone who outrages the coat of arms of the Republic or the national flag, whether by word or deed, shall be subject to six months to four years of imprisonment or a fine of fifty to three thousand pesos or both sanctions, at the judge's discretion.

##### Article 192

Anyone who makes improper use of the national coat of arms, insignia or anthem, shall be subject to three days to one year of imprisonment and a fine of twenty-five to one thousand pesos.  
TITLE SEVEN
Crimes Against Health
Title renumbered DOF 20-01-1967. Renumbered (formerly Title Eight) DOF 29-07-1970

#### CHAPTER I

Production, possession, trafficking, proselytism and other acts regarding narcotics
Chapter added DOF 14-02-1940. Chapter declared Emergency Law DOF 12-05-1945. Declaration terminated DOF 28-12-1945.  
Denomination amended DOF 14-11-1947, 08-03-1968, 31-12-1974, 10-01-1994

##### Article 193

Narcotics are considered to be stupefacients, psychotropics and other substances or plants determined by the General Health Law, international conventions and treaties of mandatory observance in Mexico and those indicated by other applicable legal provisions on the matter.  
For the purposes of this chapter, conduct related to stupefacients, psychotropics and other substances provided for in articles 237, 245, sections I, II, and III and 248 of the General Health Law, which constitute a serious problem for public health, is punishable.  
The judge, when individualizing the penalty or security measure to be imposed for the commission of any crime provided for in this chapter, shall take into account, in addition to what is established in articles 51 and 52, the quantity and type of narcotic in question, as well as the lesser or greater injury or endangerment of public health and the personal conditions of the author or participant in the act or recidivism as the case may be.
