DECREE issuing the General Law to Prevent and Punish Crimes in Matters of Kidnapping, Regulatory of section XXI of article 73 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States; and reforming, adding and repealing various provisions of the Federal Code of Criminal Procedures, the Federal Penal Code, the Federal Law against Organized Crime, the Organic Law of the Judicial Power of the Federation, the Federal Police Law, the Federal Telecommunications Law and the General Law of the National Public Security System
Published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on November 30, 2010

ARTICLE THREE. Articles 55, second and fourth paragraphs; 64, first paragraph; 85, subsection f), of section I; article 215, in its section XIII and final paragraph and section XIV are reformed; numeral 19 is added to article 24, section IV to article 85, article 180 Bis and section XVI to article 215; and articles 366 and 366 Bis are repealed, all of the Federal Penal Code, to read as follows:

……….  
TRANSITORY ARTICLES

First. This Decree shall enter into force ninety days after its publication in the Official Gazette of the Federation.  
Second. Criminal proceedings initiated before the entry into force of this Decree regarding crimes provided for therein shall continue to be processed until their conclusion in accordance with the provisions in force at the time of the commission of the acts that gave rise to them. The same shall be observed with respect to the execution of the corresponding penalties.  
Third. All legal provisions that oppose this Decree are repealed.  

Fourth. The implementation of this Decree shall be charged to the respective budgets approved for the agencies of the three levels of government obligated to comply with the provisions of this Decree.  
Fifth. The provisions relating to kidnapping crimes provided for both in the Federal Penal Code and in the local Penal Codes in force until the entry into force of this Decree shall continue to apply for acts committed during their validity. Likewise, said provisions shall continue to apply to persons prosecuted or sentenced for crimes provided for and punished by the same articles.  

Sixth. The Attorney General of the Republic and the Attorneys General of Justice of the States and of the Federal District shall have one year counted from the publication of this Decree in the Official Gazette of the Federation to issue the corresponding administrative provisions regarding protection of persons in the terms indicated by this Law, without prejudice to the protection measures they grant previously.  

Seventh. The National Public Security Council and the National Conference on Prosecution of Justice shall prepare a National Program to prevent, prosecute and punish the conduct provided for in this ordinance, independently of the program of each particular entity, having a period of six months, counted from the publication of this Decree in the Official Gazette of the Federation.  

Eighth. The reform to section XIV of article 44 of the Federal Telecommunications Law shall enter into force on the day following the publication of the Decree with respect to users of mobile telephony in any of its modalities acquired prior to the entry into force of this Decree and, with respect to new users of mobile telephony, in terms of transitory article four of the decree of reforms to said law, published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on February 9, 2009.  

Ninth. The Federal Institute of Public Defense of the Judicial Power of the Federation, within the scope of its competence, shall establish specialized areas in defense of victims of kidnapping, in the terms provided in the law on the matter.  
Tenth. For the establishment and organization of the specialized units against kidnapping referred to in this Law, the federal entities shall have the resources of the Public Security Support Fund that they have respectively received.  
Eleventh. The H. Congress of the Union may empower victims or those injured by the crimes provided for in the General Law to Prevent and Punish Crimes in Matters of Kidnapping, Regulatory of article 73 section XXI of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, to exercise the right regarding the exercise of criminal action before the judicial authority for the crime of kidnapping, in the law on the matter to be issued for that purpose.  

Twelfth. Within a period of one hundred eighty days counted from the entry into force of this Law, the necessary adjustments shall be made to the applicable provisions so that the corresponding resources are allocated to the Fund referred to in article 38 of the General Law to Prevent and Punish Crimes in Matters of Kidnapping, Regulatory of section XXI of article 73 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States.  

Mexico City,  
F. D., October 7, 2010.- Sen. Manlio Fabio Beltrones Rivera, President.- Rep. Jorge Carlos Ramírez Marín, President.- Sen. Martha Leticia Sosa Govea, Secretary.- Rep. María de Jesús Aguirre Maldonado, Secretary.- Signatures."

In compliance with the provisions of section I of Article 89 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, and for its due publication and observance, I issue this Decree at the Residence of the Federal Executive Power, in Mexico City, Federal District, on November 29, 2010.- Felipe de Jesús Calderón Hinojosa.- Signature.- The Secretary of the Interior, José Francisco Blake Mora.- Signature.

This excerpt sits under: FEDERAL PENAL CODE > FIRST BOOK PRELIMINARY TITLE > FIRST TITLE > CHAPTER III

##### FIRST ARTICLE

This decree shall enter into force on the day following its publication in the Official Gazette of the Federation, except as provided in the third, fourth and sixth transitory articles below.  
SECOND ARTICLE. Within a period not exceeding six months from the entry into force of this decree, the Plenary shall publish the technical criteria referred to in article 24, section XVIII bis, subsections a) through j) of the Federal Economic Competition Law.  
THIRD ARTICLE. Article 28, first and second paragraphs, of the Federal Economic Competition Law shall enter into force once the current term of the President of the Commission concludes.  
FOURTH ARTICLE. Investigations, verification visits, proceedings and any other matters that are in process at the entry into force of this Decree, shall be processed in accordance with the provisions in force at the time of their commencement.  
Infractions and crimes committed prior to the date of entry into force of this Decree, shall be sanctioned in accordance with the Law in force at the time of their commission.  
FIFTH ARTICLE. The resources necessary for the implementation of this Decree shall be charged to the authorized budget of the Federal Competition Commission.  
SIXTH ARTICLE. The amendment to article 39 of the Federal Economic Competition Law shall enter into force once the specialized courts on economic competition matters are established by the Judicial Power of the Federation and the procedural rules applicable to the ordinary administrative trial are issued in the corresponding legal provisions, within a period not exceeding 180 calendar days from the entry into force of this Decree.  

SEVENTH ARTICLE. Provisions that are contrary to this Decree are hereby repealed.  

Mexico City,  
Federal District, April 28, 2011.- Rep. Jorge Carlos Ramirez Marin, President.- Sen. Manlio Fabio Beltrones Rivera, President.- Rep. Maria de Jesus Aguirre Maldonado, Secretary.- Sen. Martha Leticia Sosa Govea, Secretary.- Signatures."

FIRST. This Decree shall enter into force on the day following its publication in the Official Gazette of the Federation.  
SECOND. The amendments to the General Population Law shall enter into force on the day following its publication in the Official Gazette of the Federation, except for the repeal of sections VII and VIII of article 3 and articles 7 through 75, which shall enter into force once the Regulations of the Migration Law are in effect.  

The amendments to the Law on Religious Associations and Public Worship, the Foreign Investment Law and the General Tourism Law, shall enter into force once the Regulations of the Migration Law are in effect.  
THIRD. References made in other laws and legal provisions to the General Population Law with respect to migration matters, shall be understood as referring to the Migration Law.  
FOURTH. Resolutions issued by the migration authority during the validity of the provisions of the General Population Law that are repealed, shall have their full legal effects.

DECREE amending and adding various provisions of the Federal Code of Criminal Procedures, the Organic Law of the Judicial Power of the Federation, the Organic Law of the Office of the Attorney General of the Republic and the Federal Penal Code.

Article Fourth. The third and fourth paragraphs are added to article 51 of the Federal Penal Code, to read as follows:
……….  
Transitory Provisions

First.- This Decree shall enter into force the day following its publication in the Official Gazette of the Federation.  
Second.- The Head of the Federal Executive Power shall have a term of 180 calendar days to issue the amendments to the Regulations of the Organic Law of the Office of the Attorney General of the Republic, in order to establish the administrative unit that shall have jurisdiction over federal crimes committed against any journalist, person or facility that threaten the right to information or the freedoms of expression or press, as well as for those common jurisdiction crimes that are attracted in accordance with the provisions of articles 73, section XXI, second paragraph, of the Constitution and 10 of the Federal Code of Criminal Procedures.  

Third.- Until the amendments to the Regulations of the Organic Law of the Office of the Attorney General of the Republic referred to in the preceding transitory article are issued, the Special Prosecutor's Office for Attention to Crimes Committed against Freedom of Expression, of the Office of the Attorney General of the Republic shall exercise the powers established in this Decree.

DECREE issuing the Federal Law on Environmental Liability and amending, adding and repealing various provisions of the General Law on Ecological Balance and Environmental Protection, the General Wildlife Law, the General Law for the Prevention and Comprehensive Management of Waste, the General Law on Sustainable Forestry Development, the National Waters Law, the Federal Penal Code, the Navigation and Maritime Commerce Law and the General Law on National Assets.  
Published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on June 7, 2013

ARTICLE SEVENTH.- The first paragraph, section I and the fifth paragraph of article 421 are amended; and a sixth paragraph is added to article 421, of the Federal Penal Code, to read as follows:
……….  
TRANSITORY PROVISIONS

FIRST.- This Decree shall enter into force thirty days following its publication in the Official Gazette of the Federation.  
SECOND.- The Environmental Liability Fund must be established and its bases and operating rules, prepared and approved within one hundred eighty days following the entry into force of this Decree.  
THIRD.- The District Courts specialized in environmental matters must be established within a maximum term of two years counted from the entry into force of this Decree. The specialized jurisdiction in environmental matters may be granted to the District Courts in operation in each jurisdictional circuit or according to the provisions of the Federal Judicial Council, without this implying the creation of new jurisdictional bodies. The personnel of each of said District Courts shall receive specialized training in environmental regulations.  

Mexico City,  
Federal District, April 25, 2013.- Sen. Ernesto Cordero Arroyo, President.- Rep. Francisco Arroyo Vieyra, President.- Sen. Rosa Adriana Díaz Lizama, Secretary.- Rep. Javier Orozco Gómez, Secretary.- Signatures."

DECREE amending and adding various provisions of the Federal Penal Code; the Federal Code of Criminal Procedures; the Federal Law against Organized Crime; the Federal Fiscal Code and the Federal Law on Extinction of Ownership, Regulatory of Article 22 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States.  
Published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on March 14, 2014

##### FIRST ARTICLE

Articles 139; 148 Bis; 148 Quáter; 170 and 400 Bis are reformed; and CHAPTER VI BIS entitled "Financing of Terrorism" is added to Title One of Book Two with articles 139 Quáter and 139 Quinquies; article 368 Quinquies is added, and articles 400 Bis 1 are added, within CHAPTER II, Twenty-Third Title, Book Two, which refers to "Operations with Resources of Illicit Origin", of the Federal Penal Code, to read as follows:
………
Transitional Provisions

FIRST. This Decree shall enter into force on the day following its publication in the Official Gazette of the Federation. SECOND. The legal provisions that establish measures and procedures to prevent and detect acts, omissions or operations of those established in article 400 Bis of the Federal Penal Code, as well as acts or omissions that could favor, provide aid, assistance or cooperation of any kind for the commission of the crimes provided for in articles 139 or 148 Bis of the same Code established in other laws, shall be understood henceforth as also applicable as appropriate, for the prevention, identification and prosecution of the conducts provided for in article 139 Quáter of the aforementioned Federal Penal Code. THIRD. Those who have committed any of the crimes provided for in articles 139; 148 Bis; 148 Quáter; 170 and 400 Bis, which are reformed by virtue of this Decree prior to its entry into force, including those who are within any stage of the procedures provided for in the Federal Code of Criminal Procedures, shall continue to be subject to the provisions in force at the time the crime was committed and those sentenced shall serve their penalties and sanctions in the terms in which they were imposed. Mexico City,  
Federal District, February 11, 2014.- Rep. Ricardo Anaya Cortés, President.- Sen. Raúl Cervantes Andrade, President.- Rep. Merilyn Gómez Pozos, Secretary.- Sen. Lilia Guadalupe Merodio Reza, Secretary.- Signatures."

DECREE by which the Federal Economic Competition Law is issued and various articles of the Federal Penal Code are reformed and added. Published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on May 23, 2014

SECOND ARTICLE.- Article 254 bis is reformed and article 254 bis 1 is added to the Federal Penal Code, to read as follows:
......... TRANSITIONAL PROVISIONS

First. This Decree shall enter into force forty-five calendar days following its publication in the Official Gazette of the Federation. Second. The Federal Economic Competition Law published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on December 24, 1992 is abrogated. Procedures that are in process at the entry into force of this Decree shall be processed in accordance with the provisions in force at the time of their initiation, before the administrative units established by the organic statute issued pursuant to the following transitional provision. The resolutions issued in said procedures may only be challenged through amparo proceedings, in accordance with the provisions of article 28 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States. Third. For the case of the appointment of the first holder as Investigating Authority, with regard to the requirement established in section VII of article 31 of this Law, it must be understood in the sense that during the three years prior to his appointment, he must not have held any employment, position or managerial function or have represented in any way the interests of any Economic Agent that has been subject to any of the procedures provided for in the Federal Economic Competition Law published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on December 24, 1992, in force until before the entry into force of this Decree. Fourth. The Plenary of the Commission must adapt its Organic Statute to the provisions of this Decree within a period not exceeding thirty days from its entry into force. Until the adaptation is made, the Organic Statute in force at the time of entry into force of this Decree shall continue to apply, insofar as it does not conflict with it. Fifth. Within thirty days following the entry into force of this Decree, the Chamber of Deputies of the Congress of the Union must integrate a technical working group, with the purpose of analyzing and formulating proposals for adjustments to the criminal legislation in force, in the matter subject of this Decree. The working group must present the corresponding proposals within sixty days following its installation. Sixth. Within a period not exceeding six months from the entry into force of this Decree, the Plenary shall publish the Regulatory Provisions referred to in article 12, section XXII of the Federal Economic Competition Law. Seventh. Within one year from the entry into force of this Decree, the Congress of the Union must make adjustments to the legal framework in order to harmonize it with the principles on competition and free concurrence provided for in article 28 of the Constitution. For this purpose, the Congress of the Union may request an opinion from the Federal Economic Competition Commission. Mexico City,  
Federal District, April 29, 2014.- Sen. Raúl Cervantes Andrade, President.- Rep. José González Morfín, President.- Sen. Rosa Adriana Díaz Lizama, Secretary.- Rep. Javier Orozco Gómez, Secretary.- Signatures."

DECREE by which the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law, and the Law of the Public Broadcasting System of the Mexican State are issued; and various provisions on telecommunications and broadcasting are reformed, added and repealed. Published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on July 14, 2014

NINTH ARTICLE.- The first paragraph of article 140; article 145; section II of article 167; article 178 Bis; the first paragraph of article 212; section III of article 214 are reformed; and article 166 Bis of the Federal Penal Code is added, to read as follows:

………
TRANSITIONAL PROVISIONS

FIRST. This Decree shall enter into force thirty calendar days following its publication in the Official Gazette of the Federation, without prejudice to the provisions of the following transitional provisions. SECOND. The Federal Telecommunications Law and the Federal Radio and Television Law are abrogated. Those provisions of the General Law of Communication Routes that are contrary to the provisions of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law issued by virtue of this Decree are rendered without effect. THIRD. The regulatory and administrative provisions and the official Mexican standards in force shall continue to apply until new ordinances are issued to replace them, except insofar as they are contrary to the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law issued by virtue of this Decree. FOURTH. The Federal Telecommunications Institute must adapt its organic statute to the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law within sixty calendar days following the entry into force of this Decree. FIFTH. The Federal Executive must issue, within one hundred eighty calendar days following the issuance of this Decree, the regulatory provisions and guidelines on content established in the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law issued by virtue of this Decree. Broadcasting concessionaires and restricted television or audio concessionaires may not promote video games that have not been classified in accordance with applicable regulations, which must be issued by the Federal Executive within the period referred to in the preceding paragraph. SIXTH. The attention, processing and resolution of matters and procedures that have begun prior to the entry into force of this Decree shall be carried out in the terms established in the Seventh Transitional Article of the Decree by which various provisions of articles 6, 7, 27, 28, 73, 78, 94 and 105 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States on telecommunications are reformed and added, published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on June 11, 2013. The foregoing without prejudice to the provisions of the Twentieth Transitional Provision of this Decree. SEVENTH. Without prejudice to what is established in the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law issued by virtue of the Decree, in the law and in the regulations issued for that purpose by the Federal Telecommunications Institute, concessions and permits granted prior to the entry into force of this Decree shall be maintained in the terms and conditions set forth in the respective titles or

permits until their termination, unless authorization is obtained to provide services additional to those that are the object of their concession or they have transitioned to the single concession provided for in the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law, in which case, the terms and conditions established by the Federal Telecommunications Institute shall apply. In the case of radio spectrum concessions, they may not be modified with regard to the term of the concession, the authorized coverage and the number of Megahertz granted, nor may the conditions of doing or not doing provided for in the original concession title and that were determinative for the granting of the concession be modified. Requests for extension of sound broadcasting concessions submitted prior to the date of termination of the original term established in the corresponding titles shall be resolved in accordance with the provisions of article 114 of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law, without the deadline provided for the extension request in question being applicable. Paragraph added OGF 06-15-2018

EIGHTH. Except as provided in the Tenth and Eleventh Transitional articles of this Decree, current concessionaires may obtain authorization from the Federal Telecommunications Institute to provide services additional to those that are the object of their concession or to transition to the single concession, provided they are in compliance with the obligations provided for in the laws and in their concession titles. Concessionaires that hold radio spectrum concessions must pay the corresponding considerations in terms of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law. Concessionaires that hold several concession titles, in addition to being able to transition to the single concession, may consolidate their titles into a single concession. NINTH. As long as there is a preponderant economic agent in the telecommunications and broadcasting sectors, in order to promote competition and develop viable competitors in the long term, concentrations carried out between economic agents holding concessions, nor the assignments of concessions and changes of control derived from them, that meet the following requirements, shall not require authorization from the Federal Telecommunications Institute:

a. They generate a sectoral reduction of the Dominance Index "ID", provided that the Hirschman-Herfindahl index "IHH" does not increase by more than two hundred points; b. They result in the economic agent having a sectoral participation percentage of less than twenty percent; c. That the preponderant economic agent in the sector in which the concentration takes place does not participate in said concentration, and
d. They do not have the effect of diminishing, damaging or impeding free competition and concurrence in the corresponding sector. By Hirschman-Herfindahl Index "IHH" is understood the sum of the squares of the participations of each economic agent (IHH=i qi2), in the corresponding sector, measured for the case of the telecommunications sector based on the indicator of number of subscribers and users of telecommunications services, and for the broadcasting sector based on audience. This index can take values between zero and ten thousand. To calculate the Dominance Index "ID", the percentage contribution hi of each economic agent to the IHH index defined in the preceding paragraph will first be determined (hi = 100xqi2/IHH). Then the value of ID will be calculated by applying the Hirschman-Herfindahl formula, but now using the contributions hi instead of the participations qi (that is, ID=i hi2). This index also varies between zero and ten thousand. Economic agents must submit to the Federal Telecommunications Institute, within 10 days following the concentration, a written notice that will contain the information referred to in article 89 of the Federal Economic Competition Law referred to the corresponding sector as well as the elements of conviction that demonstrate that the concentration complies with the preceding subsections. The Institute shall investigate such concentrations within a period not exceeding ninety calendar days and if it finds that there is substantial power in the market of telecommunications networks that provide voice, data or video services or in radio and television according to the corresponding sector, it may impose the necessary measures to protect and promote free competition and concurrence in said market, in accordance with the provisions of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law and the Federal Economic Competition Law without prejudice to the concentrations referred to in this article. The measures imposed by the Institute shall be extinguished once preponderant economic agents are authorized to provide additional services. TENTH. Preponderant economic agents and concessionaires whose concession titles contain any express prohibition or restriction to provide certain services, prior to the initiation of the procedure to obtain authorization to provide additional services, shall accredit before the Federal Telecommunications Institute and the latter shall supervise the effective compliance with the obligations provided for in the Decree by which various provisions of articles 6, 7, 27, 28, 73, 78, 94 and 105 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States on telecommunications are reformed and added, published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on June 11, 2013, of the Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law, as well as of the Federal Economic Competition Law, their concession titles and applicable administrative provisions, in accordance with the following:

I. Preponderant economic agents must accredit before the Federal Telecommunications Institute that they are in effective compliance with the foregoing and with the measures issued by the Federal Telecommunications Institute itself referred to in sections III and IV of the Eighth Transitional Article of the aforementioned Decree. For this purpose, the Federal Telecommunications Institute shall establish the form and terms for submitting the respective information and documentation; II. The preponderant economic agent must be in effective compliance with the measures referred to in section I above for at least eighteen months continuously; III. After the period referred to in the preceding section has elapsed and provided that it continues to comply with the provisions of section I above, the Federal Telecommunications Institute shall resolve and issue an opinion certifying that effective compliance with the referred obligations was given, and

IV. Once the concessionaire has obtained the compliance certification, it may request authorization for the additional service from the Federal Telecommunications Institute. The provisions of this article shall also be applicable in the event that the respective agents and concessionaires choose to transition to the single concession. The provisions of this article shall not be applicable after five years have elapsed from the entry into force of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law, provided that the preponderant economic agent in the telecommunications sector is in compliance with the Eighth Transitional Article of this Decree, with the measures that have been imposed on it pursuant to the provisions of sections III and IV of the Eighth Transitional Article of the Decree by which various provisions of articles 6, 7, 27, 28, 73, 78, 94 and 105 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States on telecommunications are reformed and added, published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on June 11, 2013, and with those that the Federal Telecommunications Institute has imposed on it in the terms of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law. ELEVENTH. The processing of the application referred to in the preceding article shall be subject to the following:  
I. Preponderant economic agents and concessionaires whose concession titles contain any express prohibition or restriction to provide certain services must comply with the provisions of the guidelines of the Federal Telecommunications Institute in terms of the Fourth Transitional Article of the Decree by which various provisions of articles 6, 7, 27, 28, 73, 78, 94 and 105 of the Constitution on telecommunications are reformed and added, published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on June 11, 2013; II. When submitting the application, said agents and concessionaires must accompany the compliance opinion referred to in section III of the preceding article, submit the information determined by the Federal Telecommunications Institute regarding the services it intends to provide; III. The Federal Telecommunications Institute shall resolve on the merits of the application within sixty calendar days following its submission, based on the general guidelines it issues for that purpose and shall determine the applicable considerations. Once the period indicated in the preceding paragraph has elapsed without the Institute having resolved the corresponding application, it shall be deemed to be in the negative, and
IV. In processing the application, the Federal Telecommunications Institute must ensure that the granting of the authorization does not generate adverse effects to competition and free concurrence. It shall be understood that adverse effects to competition and free concurrence are generated, among other factors considered by the Federal Telecommunications Institute, when:  
a. Said authorization may have the effect of increasing the participation in the corresponding sector of the preponderant economic agent or of the economic interest group to which the concessionaires whose concession titles contain a prohibition or restriction to provide certain services belong, with respect to the participation determined by the Federal Telecommunications Institute in the resolution by which it was declared a preponderant economic agent in the corresponding sector. b. The authorization of additional services has the effect of conferring substantial power in the relevant market to any of the concessionaires or members of the preponderant economic agent or of the concessionaires whose concession titles contain a prohibition or restriction to provide certain services in the corresponding sector. The provisions of this article shall be applicable in the event that the respective agents and concessionaires choose to transition to the single concession, and shall be independent of the economic sanctions that proceed in accordance with the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law. TWELFTH. The preponderant economic agent in the telecommunications sector may opt at any time for the scheme provided for in article 276 of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law or exercise the right established by this article. The preponderant economic agent in the telecommunications sector may submit to the Federal Telecommunications Institute a plan based on a real, concrete situation and with respect to determined persons, which includes where applicable, structural separation, total or partial divestiture of assets, rights, corporate shares or shares or any combination of the foregoing options in order to reduce its national participation below fifty percent of the telecommunications sector referred to in section III of the Eighth Transitional Article of the Decree by which various provisions of articles 6, 7, 27, 28, 73, 78, 94 and 105 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States on telecommunications are reformed and added, published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on June 11, 2013, in accordance with the variables and measurement parameters used by the Federal Telecommunications Institute in the corresponding preponderance declaration, and provided that the execution of said plan generates conditions of effective competition in the markets that make up said sector in accordance with the Federal Economic Competition Law. In the event that the preponderant economic agent exercises this option, the following shall apply:

I. When submitting the plan referred to in the preceding paragraph, the preponderant economic agent must state in writing that it adheres to the provisions of this article and that it accepts its terms and conditions; it must also accompany the necessary information and documentation that allows the Federal Telecommunications Institute to know and analyze the proposed plan; II. In the event that the Federal Telecommunications Institute considers that the information submitted is insufficient, within 20 business days following the submission of the plan, it shall warn the preponderant economic agent to submit the missing information within 20 business days. In the event that the preponderant economic agent does not respond to the warning within the indicated period or that in the judgment of the Institute the documentation or information submitted is not sufficient or suitable to analyze the proposed plan, a second warning may be made in the terms indicated above and in the event that it does not comply with this last warning, the plan shall be deemed not submitted, without prejudice to the economic agent being able to submit a new plan proposal in terms of this article; III. Having responded to the warning in the terms formulated, the Federal Telecommunications Institute shall analyze, evaluate and, where appropriate, approve the proposed plan within one hundred twenty calendar days following. In the event that the Institute deems it necessary, it may extend said period up to two times and up to ninety calendar days each. To approve said plan, the Federal Telecommunications Institute must determine that it effectively reduces the national participation of the preponderant economic agent below fifty percent in the telecommunications sector referred to in section III of the Eighth Transitional Article of the aforementioned Decree, that it generates conditions of effective competition in the markets that make up said sector in the terms of the Federal Economic Competition Law and that it is not intended to or has the effect of affecting or reducing existing social coverage. The plan must result in the participation in the sector that the preponderant agent decreases being transferred to one or more other economic agents different from and independent of the preponderant economic agent. Upon approving the plan, the Federal Telecommunications Institute must

ensure the effective separation and independence of those agents and must establish the necessary terms and conditions so that this situation is duly safeguarded; IV. In the event that the Federal Telecommunications Institute approves the plan, the preponderant economic agent in the telecommunications sector shall have a period of up to ten business days to state that it accepts the plan and expressly consents to the tariffs that derive from the application of subsections a) and b) of the second paragraph of article 131 of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law, and sections VI to VIII of this article. Once the plan is accepted by the preponderant economic agent, it may not be modified and must be executed in its terms, without said agent being able to exercise again the benefit granted by this article and without prejudice to being able to opt for the provisions of article 276 of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law; V. The plan must be executed during the 365 calendar days following its acceptance in terms of section IV. The economic agents involved in the plan must report with the periodicity established by the Federal Telecommunications Institute on the execution process of the plan. In the event that the preponderant economic agent proves that the failure to comply with the plan within the referred period is due to causes not attributable to it, it may request an extension from the Federal Telecommunications Institute, which may be granted for a period of up to 120 calendar days, on a single occasion and provided that said causes are duly justified; VI. From the date on which the preponderant economic agent in the telecommunications sector has accepted the plan and during the period referred to in the preceding section, the reciprocal traffic compensation agreements referred to in the first paragraph of article 131 of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law shall be provisionally applied between the preponderant economic agent in the telecommunications sector and the other concessionaires, and the tariffs derived from the application of subsections a) and b) of the second paragraph of said article shall be suspended between them; VII. The Federal Telecommunications Institute shall certify that the plan has been effectively executed within the period indicated in section V of this article. For this purpose, within 5 business days following the end of the execution period or, where appropriate, the end of the corresponding extension, the Federal Telecommunications Institute must initiate studies demonstrating that its execution generated conditions of effective competition in the markets that make up the telecommunications sector, in accordance with the Federal Economic Competition Law. Once the certification referred to in the preceding paragraph is granted, the traffic compensation agreements referred to in the first paragraph of article 131 of the aforementioned Law shall be applied generally for all concessionaires; VIII. In the event that the plan is not executed within the period referred to in section V or, where appropriate, at the end of the corresponding extension, or the Federal Telecommunications Institute denies the certification referred to in the preceding section or determines that full compliance with said plan was not given in the approved terms, the reciprocal traffic compensation agreements and the suspension of the tariffs referred to in subsections a) and b) of article 131 of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law shall be rendered without effect between the preponderant economic agent in the telecommunications sector and the other concessionaires, and their application shall be retroactive to the date on which the suspension began, with said agent having to restore to the other concessionaires the amounts corresponding to the application of the aforementioned tariffs. In this case, the aforementioned concessionaires may offset the amounts to be restored against other amounts owed to the preponderant economic agent; IX. The Federal Telecommunications Institute shall authorize the economic agent that proposed the plan and the resulting economic agents or that are part of said plan, to provide services

additional to those that are the object of their concession or their transition to the single concession model, from the time it certifies that the plan has been effectively executed and provided that the execution of said plan generates conditions of effective competition in the markets that make up the telecommunications sector in accordance with the Federal Economic Competition Law; X. Once the Federal Telecommunications Institute certifies that the approved plan has been effectively executed, it shall proceed to extinguish:  
a. The resolutions by which it has determined the economic agent as preponderant in the telecommunications sector as well as the asymmetric measures it has imposed on it in the terms of the provisions of section III and IV of the Eighth Article of the aforementioned Decree, and
b. The resolutions by which it has determined the economic agent with substantial power in any market, as well as the specific measures it has imposed on it. THIRTEENTH. The Federal Executive through the Ministry of Communications and Transportation shall carry out actions aimed at installing the shared public telecommunications network referred to in the Sixteenth transitional article of the Decree by which various provisions of articles 6, 7, 27, 28, 73, 78, 94 and 105 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States on telecommunications are reformed and added, published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on June 11, 2013.In the event that the Federal Executive requires frequency bands from the spectrum freed by the transition to Digital Terrestrial Television (700 MHz band) to grow and strengthen the shared network referred to in the preceding paragraph, the Federal Telecommunications Institute shall grant them directly, provided that said network remains under the control of a public entity or agency or under a public-private partnership scheme. FOURTEENTH. The Federal Telecommunications Institute must implement a professional service system within one hundred eighty calendar days following the entry into force of this Decree, which must contain, among other aspects, recognition of the rights of workers of the Federal Telecommunications Commission who are certified as professional service workers. FIFTEENTH. The Federal Telecommunications Institute must install its Advisory Council within one hundred eighty calendar days following the entry into force of this Decree. SIXTEENTH. The Ministry of Communications and Transportation must establish mechanisms to carry out the coordination provided for in Article 9, Section V of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law, within one hundred eighty calendar days following the entry into force of this Decree. SEVENTEENTH. Broadcasting permits that are in force or in the process of renewal upon the entry into force of this Decree must transition to the corresponding concession regime within one year of the entry into force of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law, under the terms established by the Institute. Permits that have been granted to the powers of the Union, the states, the governing bodies of the Federal District, municipalities, autonomous constitutional bodies and public higher education institutions must transition to the public use concession regime, while the rest of the permits granted must do so to the social use concession regime. To transition to the corresponding concession regime, permit holders must submit an application to the Federal Telecommunications Institute, which shall resolve accordingly within ninety business days. Until the transition takes place, said permits shall be governed by the provisions of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law for public or social use concessions, as applicable. In case of failure to comply with this article, the permits shall terminate. EIGHTEENTH. The Federal Telecommunications Institute must issue within one hundred eighty days following the entry into force of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law, the work program to reorganize the radio spectrum for radio and television stations referred to in subsection b) of Section V of the Seventeenth Transitory Article of the Decree reforming and adding various provisions of Articles 6, 7, 27, 28, 73, 78, 94 and 105 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, on telecommunications matters, published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on June 11, 2013. In determining the work program, the Institute shall seek the development of the relevant radio market, the migration of the largest possible number of concessionaires' stations from AM to FM band, the strengthening of competitive conditions and continuity in the provision of services. NINETEENTH. The digital terrestrial transition shall culminate on December 31, 2015. The Federal Executive, through the Ministry of Communications and Transportation, shall implement programs and actions linked to the digital terrestrial television transition policy, for the delivery or distribution of receiving equipment or decoders referred to in the third paragraph of the Fifth Transitory Article of the Decree reforming and adding various provisions of Articles 6, 7, 27, 28, 73, 78, 94 and 105 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, on telecommunications matters, published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on June 11, 2013. The Federal Telecommunications Institute must conclude the transmission of analog broadcast television signals throughout the country no later than December 31, 2015, once a penetration level of ninety percent of low-income households defined by the Ministry of Social Development is reached, with receivers or decoders suitable for receiving digital broadcast television signals. For the foregoing, the Federal Telecommunications Institute must conclude analog broadcast television signals in advance of December 31, 2015, by coverage area of said signals, once the penetration level referred to in the preceding paragraph is reached in the corresponding area. The Ministry of Communications and Transportation and the Federal Telecommunications Institute shall conduct dissemination campaigns for the delivery or distribution of equipment and for the conclusion of analog television signal transmission, respectively. Broadcast television concessionaires and permit holders shall be obligated to make all necessary investments and installations to transition to digital terrestrial television no later than December 31, 2015. The Federal Telecommunications Institute shall monitor due compliance with the aforementioned obligation. Those permit holders or concessionaires of public or social use, including community and indigenous ones, that provide broadcasting services and are not in a position to begin digital transmissions by December 31, 2015, must, prior to that date, notify the Federal Telecommunications Institute, under the terms provided in Article 157 of the Federal

Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law so that they may be authorized to temporarily suspend their transmissions or, as applicable, reduce their apparent radiated power so that the continuity program referred to in the following paragraph of this article may be applied to them. The deadlines authorized by the Institute shall in no case exceed December 31, 2016. Paragraph added OGF 12-18-2015

In the event that by the dates of early conclusion of analog broadcast television signals by coverage area or by December 31, 2015, current broadcast television stations with an apparent radiated power less than or equal to 1 kW for VHF channels and 10 kW for UHF channels are not transmitting digital terrestrial television signals and/or the penetration level indicated in the third and fourth paragraphs of this article has not been reached, either in any region, locality or throughout the country; the Federal Telecommunications Institute must establish a program so that the population continues to receive this public service of general interest in the respective areas, while digital transmissions begin and/or the penetration levels indicated in this article are reached. The holders of the stations must make the necessary investments and installations according to the deadlines provided in the program. In no case shall actions derived from this program exceed December 31, 2016. Paragraph amended OGF 12-18-2015

Legal, administrative or regulatory provisions that oppose this transitory provision are hereby repealed. TWENTIETH. The Federal Telecommunications Institute shall apply Article 131 of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law and others that are applicable in matters of interconnection under the terms thereof, and shall guarantee due compliance with the obligations established in said provisions, which shall be enforceable without prejudice to and independent of whether upon the entry into force of the Law, it had already determined the existence of a preponderant economic agent and imposed necessary measures to prevent competition and free competition from being affected in accordance with Section III of the Eighth Transitory Article of the Decree reforming and adding various provisions of Articles 6, 7, 27, 28, 73, 78, 94 and 105 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, on telecommunications matters, published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on June 11, 2013. For purposes of the provisions of subsection b) of Article 131 of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law, and until the concessionaires referred to in that subsection agree on the corresponding interconnection rates or, as applicable, the Institute resolves any dispute regarding said rates, those currently in effect shall remain in force, except in the case of the economic agent referred to in the second paragraph of Article 131 of the cited Law, to which subsection a) of the same article shall apply. TWENTY-FIRST. For the attention, promotion and supervision of user rights provided for in the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law and in the Federal Consumer Protection Law, PROFECO must create a specialized area with a level no lower than Deputy Attorney General's Office, as well as the necessary structure for it, according to the budget approved by the Chamber of Deputies for such purpose. TWENTY-SECOND. The Federal Telecommunications Institute must issue the general administrative provisions referred to in Title Eight of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law within a maximum period of ninety calendar days from the entry into force of this Decree. TWENTY-THIRD. The budgetary impact generated by the entry into force of this Decree in matters of personal services, as well as the establishment of new powers

and activities under the responsibility of the Federal Telecommunications Institute, shall be covered with the budget annually approved by the Chamber of Deputies for said body. TWENTY-FOURTH. In accordance with the provisions of the Fifteenth, Sixteenth and Seventeenth Transitory Articles of the Decree reforming and adding various provisions of Articles 6, 7, 27, 28, 73, 78, 94 and 105 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, on telecommunications matters, published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on June 11, 2013, the last paragraph of Article 14 of the Revenue Law of the Federation for Fiscal Year 2014 is hereby repealed. TWENTY-FIFTH. The provisions of Section V of Article 118 of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law shall enter into force on January 1, 2015, so that from said date concessionaires of public telecommunications networks that provide fixed, mobile or both services may not make national long distance charges to their users for calls they make to any national destination. Without prejudice to the foregoing, concessionaires must carry out the consolidation of all existing local service areas in the country in accordance with the guidelines issued for such purpose by the Federal Telecommunications Institute. Each concessionaire must assume the costs arising from said consolidation. Likewise, the Federal Telecommunications Institute, within one hundred eighty days following the entry into force of this Decree, must define the interconnection points to the public telecommunications network of the preponderant economic agent or with substantial power. Administrative resolutions that have been issued shall be without effect insofar as they oppose the provisions of this transitory provision. Concessionaires shall maintain the numbering that has been assigned to them in order to use it for intelligent network services in their reverse charge modalities and other special services, such as 900 numbers. TWENTY-SIXTH. The Federal Executive must submit to the Senate of the Republic or, as applicable, to the Permanent Commission, the proposal for appointment of the President of the Public Broadcasting System of the Mexican State within thirty calendar days following the entry into force of this Decree. The Senate or, as applicable, the Permanent Commission must appoint the President of the System within thirty calendar days following the date on which it receives the proposal from the Federal Executive. TWENTY-SEVENTH. The representatives of the Secretaries of State who comprise the Board of Governors of the Public System of the Mexican State must be appointed within sixty calendar days following the entry into force of this Decree. TWENTY-EIGHTH. The appointment of the members of the Citizens' Council of the Public Broadcasting System of the Mexican State must be made within sixty calendar days following the entry into force of this Decree. TWENTY-NINTH. The President of the Public Broadcasting System of the Mexican State shall submit to the Board of Governors, for its approval, the draft Organic Statute within ninety calendar days following his appointment. THIRTIETH. Upon the entry into force of this Decree, the decentralized body called Audiovisual Media Promotion Body is transformed into the Public Broadcasting System of the Mexican State, which shall have the human, budgetary, financial and material resources of the aforementioned body. Until the Organic Statute of the Public Broadcasting System of the Mexican State is issued, the Organic Statute of the Audiovisual Media Promotion Body shall continue to apply, insofar as it does not oppose the Law of the Public Broadcasting System of the Mexican State. The labor rights of the personnel of the Audiovisual Media Promotion Body shall be respected in accordance with the law. THIRTY-FIRST. The human, budgetary, financial and material resources of the Audiovisual Media Promotion Body shall become part of the Public Broadcasting System of the Mexican State once its President is appointed, without prejudice to the labor rights of its workers. THIRTY-SECOND. The Ministry of the Interior must coordinate with the corresponding authorities for the exercise of the powers that the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law establishes in matters of monitoring. The Chamber of Deputies must allocate the necessary resources to guarantee the adequate exercise of the powers referred to in this transitory provision. THIRTY-THIRD. The Federal Telecommunications Institute shall issue the guidelines referred to in Section III of Article 158 of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law within a period not exceeding 180 calendar days from the day following the entry into force of this Decree. THIRTY-FOURTH. The Chamber of Deputies must allocate to the Public Broadcasting System of the Mexican State economic resources consistent with its objectives and functions, for which it must consider:  
I. Its growth plans; II. Its operating expenses, and
III. Its financial balance. THIRTY-FIFTH. With the exception of the provisions of the Twentieth Transitory Article, by which the Federal Telecommunications Institute is obligated to apply Article 131 of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law issued by virtue of this Decree and others that are applicable in matters of interconnection under the terms thereof, the administrative resolutions that the Federal Telecommunications Institute may have issued prior to the entry into force of this Decree in matters of preponderance shall continue to have all their effects. THIRTY-SIXTH. The Federal Telecommunications Institute within 180 days after the entry into force of this Decree must conduct the corresponding studies to analyze whether it is necessary to establish mechanisms that promote and encourage concessionaires to include a programming block aimed at children in which culture, sports, environmental conservation, respect for human rights, the best interests of children, gender equality and non-discrimination are promoted. THIRTY-SEVENTH. For purposes of the justice prosecution authorities referred to in Section I of Article 190 of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law, the provisions of the Federal Telecommunications Law in matters of real-time geographic location shall remain in force until the National Code of Criminal Procedures enters into force. THIRTY-EIGHTH. The Federal Telecommunications Institute must issue within sixty business days following the entry into force of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law, the necessary administrative rules that eliminate requirements that may delay or prevent number portability and, as applicable, promote that it be done through electronic means. The rules referred to in the preceding paragraph must guarantee effective portability and that it be carried out within a period not exceeding 24 hours from the request made by the holder of the respective number. To carry out said portability, only the identification of the holder and the manifestation of will of the user shall be necessary. In the case of legal entities, the procedure must be carried out by the legal representative or attorney-in-fact who proves his capacity under the terms of applicable regulations. THIRTY-NINTH. For purposes of the provisions of Article 264 of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law, the Federal Telecommunications Institute shall initiate, without prejudice to the provisions of the Ninth Transitory Article of this Decree, within thirty calendar days after its entry into force, the corresponding investigation procedures under the terms of the Federal Economic Competition Law, in order to determine the existence of economic agents with substantial power in any of the relevant markets of the telecommunications and broadcasting sectors, among which must be included the national audio and video market associated through public telecommunications networks and, as applicable, impose the corresponding measures. FORTIETH. The preponderant economic agent in the telecommunications sector or the agent with substantial power in the corresponding relevant market shall be obligated to comply with the provisions of Articles 138, Section VIII, 208 and Sections V and VI of Article 267 of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law, from its entry into force. FORTY-FIRST. Public higher education institutions that, as of the date of entry into force of this Decree, have broadcasting media referred to in Articles 67 Section II and 76 Section II of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law, shall not receive additional budget for that purpose. FORTY-SECOND. The concession to install, operate and exploit a public telecommunications network that, under the terms of the Fifteenth Transitory Article of the Decree reforming and adding various provisions of Articles 6, 7, 27, 28, 73, 78, 94 and 105 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, on telecommunications matters, must be transferred by the Federal Electricity Commission to Telecomunicaciones de México, shall not be subject to the provisions of Articles 140 and 144 of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law, exclusively with respect to those contracts in force as of the date of publication of this Decree that have been entered into between the Federal Electricity Commission and those natural or legal persons who, according to the same Law, are to be considered as end users. Said contracts shall be transferred by the Federal Electricity Commission to Telecomunicaciones de México, together with the corresponding concession title. Telecomunicaciones de México shall transfer the aforementioned contracts in favor of other concessionaires authorized to provide services to end users within six months following the date on which they were transferred to it. In the event that there is a technical, legal or economic impediment for Telecomunicaciones de México to transfer the aforementioned contracts, they shall remain in force at most until the date indicated therein for their termination, without being able to be renewed or extended for new periods. FORTY-THIRD. Within a period that shall not exceed 36 months from the entry into force of this Decree, the signals of commercial use concessionaires that transmit broadcast television and that cover more than fifty percent of the national territory must have Mexican sign language or closed captioning in the national language in the programming they transmit from 06:00 to 24:00 hours, excluding advertising and other cases established by the Federal Telecommunications Institute, in accordance with international best practices. Federal public entities that are public use concessionaires of broadcast television shall be subject to the same obligation. FORTY-FOURTH. In relation to the obligations established in matters of accessibility for persons with disabilities referred to in the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law for audience defenders, concessionaires shall have a period of up to ninety calendar days from the entry into force of this Decree to initiate the corresponding adjustments and mechanisms. FORTY-FIFTH. The restriction on accessing infrastructure sharing of the preponderant economic agent in broadcasting, provided for in Section VII of Article 266 of the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law, shall not be applicable to the concessionaire(s) resulting from the bidding of the new digital open television networks referred to in Section II of the Eighth Transitory Article of the Decree reforming and adding various provisions of Articles 6, 7, 27, 28, 73, 78, 94 and 105 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, on telecommunications matters. Mexico City,  
Federal District, July 08, 2014.- Sen. Raúl Cervantes Andrade, President.- Rep. José González Morfín, President.- Sen. María Elena Barrera Tapia, Secretary.- Rep. Angelina Carreño Mijares, Secretary.- Signatures."

In compliance with the provisions of Section I of Article 89 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, and for its due publication and observance, I issue this Decree at the Residence of the Federal Executive Power, in Mexico City, Federal District, on the fourteenth day of July, two thousand fourteen.- Enrique Peña Nieto.- Signature.- The Secretary of the Interior, Miguel Ángel Osorio Chong.- Signature. 2014, is amended to read as follows:

Second. Upon the entry into force of this Decree, all legal, regulatory and administrative provisions that are contrary to it are repealed.  
Third. DECREE issuing the Federal Law to Prevent and Punish Crimes Committed in Hydrocarbon Matters; and amending, adding and repealing various provisions of the Federal Code of Criminal Procedures; the Federal Penal Code; the Federal Law against Organized Crime; the Federal Law on Forfeiture of Assets, regulating Article 22 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States; the Federal Fiscal Code and the National Code of Criminal Procedures.  
Published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on January 12, 2016

ARTICLE THIRD. A third paragraph is added to article 140; a final paragraph is added to article 241 and a third paragraph is added to article 243, and subsection j) of section I of article 253; sections VII and VIII of article 254, and article 368 Quater of the Federal Penal Code are repealed, to read as follows:
………
TRANSITORY

First.- This Decree shall enter into force on the day following its publication in the Official Gazette of the Federation.  
Second.- Criminal proceedings initiated before the entry into force of this Decree shall continue to be processed until their conclusion in accordance with the provisions in force at the time of the commission of the acts that gave rise to them.  

Third.- As of the entry into force of this Decree, in the event that the Federal Law to Prevent and Punish Crimes Committed in Hydrocarbon Matters contemplates a legal description of a criminal conduct that in the former Federal Penal Code or Federal Fiscal Code was contemplated as a crime and by virtue of these reforms, is denominated, penalized or aggravated in a different manner, provided that the conducts and facts respond to the description now established, the following shall apply:

I.	In proceedings initiated in which accusatory conclusions have not yet been formulated, the Federal Public Prosecutor's Office shall formulate them in accordance with the resulting transposition of the type;  
II.	In proceedings pending judgment in first and second instance, the judge or the Court, respectively, may effect the transposition of the type in accordance with the conduct that has been proven and its modalities; and
III.	The executing authority, when applying any modality of benefit for the convicted person, shall consider the penalties that have been imposed, based on the transposition of the type, according to the corresponding modalities.  
Fourth.- The pecuniary sanctions provided for in the Federal Law to Prevent and Punish Crimes Committed in Hydrocarbon Matters shall be adjusted, as appropriate, to the equivalent unit of measurement and updating provided by law in the Mexican penal system.  
Fifth.- The expenditures generated by the entry into force of this Decree for the agencies and entities of the Federal Public Administration shall be covered with the resources approved by the Chamber of Deputies in the Expenditure Budget of the Federation for the fiscal year in

question, and therefore no additional resources shall be authorized for the current fiscal year and subsequent ones.  
Mexico City,  
Federal District, December 15, 2015.- Rep. José de Jesús Zambrano Grijalva, President.- Sen. Roberto Gil Zuarth, President.- Rep. Ernestina Godoy Ramos, Secretary.- Sen. María Elena Barrera Tapia, Secretary.- Signatures."

In compliance with the provisions of Section I of Article 89 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, and for its due publication and observance, I issue this Decree at the Residence of the Federal Executive Power, in Mexico City, Federal District, on the eleventh of January, two thousand sixteen.- Enrique Peña Nieto.- Signature.- The Secretary of the Interior, Miguel Ángel Osorio Chong.- Signature.
