DECREE issuing the National Law on Criminal Execution; adding sections XXXV, XXXVI and XXXVII and a fifth paragraph, and amending the third paragraph of article 225 of the Federal Penal Code.  
Published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on June 16, 2016

##### Article First. The National Law on Criminal Enforcement is hereby issued

………
TRANSITORY

First. This Law shall enter into force on the day following its publication in the Official Gazette of the Federation.  
For the purposes set forth in the third paragraph of the second transitory article of the Decree reforming and adding various provisions of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on June 18, 2008, it is declared that this legislation incorporates the accusatory criminal procedural system and shall enter into force in accordance with the following transitory articles.  
Second. Sections III and X and the seventh paragraph of article 10; articles 26 and 27, section II of article 28; section VII of article 108; articles 146, 147, 148, 149, 150 and 151 shall enter into force one year after the publication of this Law or on the day following the publication of the Declaration issued to that effect by the Congress of the Union or the legislatures of the federal entities within the scope of their respective competences, without exceeding November 30, 2017.  

Articles 31, 32, 33, 34, 35, 36, 59, 60, 61, 75, 77, 78, 80, 82, 83, 86, 91, 92, 93, 94, 95, 96, 97,
98, 99, 128, 136, 145, 153, 165, 166, 169, 170, 171, 172, 173, 174, 175, 176, 177, 178, 179, 180, 181,
182, 183, 184, 185, 186, 187, 188, 189, 192, 193, 194, 195, 200, 201, 202, 203, 204, 205, 206 and 207
shall enter into force no later than two years after the publication of this Law or on the day following the publication of the Declaration issued to that effect by the Congress of the Union or the legislatures of the federal entities within the scope of their respective competences, without exceeding November 30, 2018.  
At the Federal level, the Congress of the Union shall issue the Declaration, upon joint request of the Coordination Council for the Implementation of the Criminal Justice System or the body that, as the case may be, is charged with coordinating the consolidation of the Criminal Justice System, and the National Conference of the Penitentiary System.  

In the case of the federal entities, the corresponding legislative body shall issue the Declaration upon request of the authority responsible for the implementation of the Criminal Justice System in each of them.  
In the federal entities where the new Criminal Justice System is in force, the corresponding legislative body must issue within the following ten days the annex to the Declaration for the commencement of validity of this Law.  
Third. As of the entry into force of this Law, the Law Establishing Minimum Standards on Social Rehabilitation of Sentenced Persons and those regulating the execution of criminal sanctions in the federal entities shall be repealed.  

Proceedings that are in process at the entry into force of this ordinance shall continue with their processing in accordance with the legislation applicable at the commencement thereof, applying the jurisdictional control mechanisms provided for in this Law, in accordance with the pro persona principle established in article 1 of the Constitution.  

As of the entry into force of this Law, all regulatory provisions that contravene it are hereby repealed.  
Fourth. As of the entry into force of this Law, the provisions contained in the Federal Criminal Code and special laws of the federation relating to partial remission of sentence, preparatory release and substitution of sentence during execution are hereby repealed.  
The federal entities must adapt their legislation in order to repeal the provisions relating to partial remission of sentence, preparatory release and substitution of sentence during execution, within the scope of their respective competences.  
The federal entities must legislate in their criminal codes on the responsibilities of liberty supervisors.  
Fifth. Within a period not exceeding one hundred eighty calendar days after the publication of this Decree, the Federation and the federal entities must publish the amendments to their laws that are necessary for the implementation of this Law, as well as the provisions of article 92, section V regarding social security.  

Upon the entry into force of this Law, in those places where its commencement is determined, both at the federal and local levels, the corresponding general administrative provisions must be in place, and the standardization of methodological, technical and procedural criteria may be provided for, for which purpose the authorities involved may coordinate.  
Sixth. The expenditures generated by reason of the entry into force of this Decree for the Judicial Branch of the Federation, the agencies and entities of the Federal Public Administration, shall be covered with charges to their budgets for the current fiscal year and subsequent ones.  

Likewise, the federal entities must make the necessary budgetary provisions and adjustments to comply with the obligations established in this Decree.  
Seventh. The Council of the Federal Judiciary, the Federal Public Defender's Institute, the Attorney General's Office, the Ministry of the Interior, the Ministry of Labor and Social Welfare, the Ministry of Economy, the Ministry of Public Education, the Ministry of Culture, the Ministry of Social Development, the Ministry of Health, the National Commission for Physical Culture and Sports, the Executive Commission for Victim Assistance and any agency or entity of the Federal Public Administration and their equivalents in the federal entities to which direct or indirect responsibilities are conferred in this Law, must provide in their programs for the adequate and correct implementation, and must establish within the respective budget projects, the necessary items to attend to the execution of those programs, infrastructure works, hiring of personnel, training and all other requirements necessary to fulfill the objectives of this Law.  

Eighth. The Coordination Council for the Implementation of the Criminal Justice System shall establish a Committee for the Implementation, Evaluation and Monitoring of the Criminal Enforcement System which shall be chaired by the National Conference of the Penitentiary System, which shall render a semi-annual report to the Coordination Council. The foregoing for the purpose of coordinating, contributing and supporting the federal authorities and the federal entities when so requested.  

The Penitentiary Authority shall have a period of four years, from the publication of this Decree, to train, adapt penitentiary establishments and their installed capacity, equip, develop information and communications technologies, as well as adapt its organizational structure. All of this in accordance with the activity plans registered with the Committee referred to in the preceding paragraph.  

The Coordination Council shall present annually before the Chambers of the Congress of the Union, an annual report on the monitoring of the implementation of the Criminal Enforcement System.  
Ninth. Within one hundred eighty calendar days following the entry into force of this Decree, the National Conference of the Penitentiary System must issue a General Agreement establishing a gradual regime by virtue of which the Penitentiary Authorities, within the scope of their competences, shall allocate special confinement spaces, within penitentiary establishments, for those sentenced for the crimes of organized crime and kidnapping, provided for in the General Law to Prevent and Punish Crimes in the Matter of Kidnapping, as well as those persons deprived of liberty who require special security measures.  

Tenth. As of the entry into force of this Decree, all persons who have been sentenced with custodial sentences for the commission of the following crimes may access, immediately and without having to satisfy the requirements established in sections IV and VII of article 141 of this Law, the benefit of early release:  
I.	The commission of the crime of theft whose value of the stolen property does not exceed 80 times the Unit of Measurement and Update, and when no type of violence was involved in the commission of the crime, or
II.	The commission of the crime of possession without purposes of commerce or supply, of Cannabis Sativa, Indica or Marijuana, contemplated in article 477 of the General Health Law, in any of its forms, derivatives or preparations, and when no type of violence was involved in the commission of the crime, nor the concurrence of more crimes.  
For such purpose, the jurisdictional authority shall require from the Penitentiary Authority the report on compliance with the requirements referred to in the preceding paragraph.  
Eleventh. The prosecutors or attorneys general of the Federation and of the federal entities, within their respective scope of competence, may request before the competent jurisdictional authority, the application of the early release benefits referred to in the tenth transitory article. Likewise, the competent judicial authorities shall process the respective procedure ex officio or at the request of the person to whom said benefit applies.  

Twelfth. The Judicial Branch of the Federation and the judicial branches of the federal entities shall issue general agreements, to determine the exceptional territorial jurisdiction of the enforcement courts for the purpose of hearing the various matters by reason of security and special measures, while the Law enters into force; for which purpose it may execute the corresponding agreements with the operating bodies of the Criminal Justice System.  

Article Second.- Sections XXXV, XXXVI and XXXVII and a fifth paragraph are added, and the third paragraph of article 225 of the Federal Criminal Code is reformed, to read as follows:
……..  
Transitory

Sole.- This Decree shall enter into force on the day following its publication in the Official Gazette of the Federation.  
Mexico City, June 14, 2016.- Sen. Roberto Gil Zuarth, President.- Rep. José de Jesús Zambrano Grijalva, President.- Sen. Hilda Esthela Flores Escalera, Secretary.- Rep. Juan Manuel Celis Aguirre, Secretary.- Signatures."

In compliance with the provisions of section I of Article 89 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, and for its due publication and observance, I issue this Decree at the Residence of the Federal Executive Power, in Mexico City, on the sixteenth day of June, two thousand sixteen.- Enrique Peña Nieto.- Signature.- The Minister of the Interior, Miguel Ángel Osorio Chong.-Signature.  

DECREE reforming, adding and repealing various provisions of the National Code of Criminal Procedures; the Federal Criminal Code; the General Law of the National Public Security System; the Federal Law for the Protection of Persons Involved in Criminal Proceedings; the General Law to Prevent and Punish Crimes in the Matter of Kidnapping, Regulatory of section XXI of Article 73 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, the Amparo Law, Regulatory of articles 103 and 107 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, the Organic Law of the Judicial Branch of the Federation, the Federal Public Defender's Law, the Federal Fiscal Code and the Law on Credit Institutions.  
Published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on June 17, 2016

Article Second.- Articles 7, section I of the third paragraph; 16; 25; 27, first and last paragraphs; 29, last paragraph; 34, first and last paragraphs; 35, fourth paragraph; 38; 40; 50 Bis, first
paragraph; 55, first and third paragraphs; 56; 64; 65, second paragraph; 71, second paragraph; 74, first
paragraph; 75; 76; 77; 87; the title of Title Fifth, Chapter I of Book First; 91; 93, fourth
paragraph; 97, first paragraph; 99; 101, second and third paragraphs; 110, first and third paragraphs; 114; 115, first paragraph; the title of Chapter VIII of Title Fifth of Book First; 225, sections IX, X, XII, XIV, XVI, XVII, XIX, XXI, XXVII, XXVIII, XXX, XXXI and XXXII are reformed; articles 11 Bis; a second paragraph to article 55, renumbering the subsequent ones in their order; a fourth paragraph to article 211 Bis 2 are added; the fifth paragraph of article 35; the fourth and sixth paragraphs of article 55; article 90 Bis and sections XI and XIII of article 225, of the Federal Criminal Code, are repealed, to read as follows:
………
Transitory

First.- This Decree shall enter into force on the day following its publication in the Official Gazette of the Federation except as provided in the following article.  
Second.- The reforms to the National Code of Criminal Procedures, the Federal Criminal Code, the General Law to Prevent and Punish Crimes in the Matter of Kidnapping, Regulatory of section XXI of article 73 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, articles 2, 13, 44 and 49 of the Federal Law for the Protection of Persons Involved in Criminal Proceedings and articles 21 in its section X, 50 Bis and 158 of the Organic Law of the Judicial Branch of the Federation, shall enter into force in terms of the provisions of the Second Transitory Article of the Decree issuing the National Code of Criminal Procedures published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on March 5, 2014.  

Proceedings that are in process, related to the modifications to the legal provisions contemplated in this Decree, shall be resolved in accordance with the provisions that gave rise to them.  
Third.- Within 180 calendar days of the entry into force of this Decree, the Federation and the federal entities within the scope of their respective competences, must have an Authority for Supervision of Precautionary Measures and Conditional Suspension of Proceedings.  
Likewise, within 30 days following the date of creation of the authorities for precautionary measures and conditional suspension of proceedings of the Federation and of the federal entities, the agreements and guidelines regulating their organization and operation must be issued.  

Fourth.- The provisions of this Decree relating to criminal enforcement shall enter into force once the legislation on the matter provided for in article 73, section XXI, subsection c) of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States enters into force.  

Fifth.- In the case of those measures depriving personal liberty or preventive detention that have been decreed by order of judicial authority during proceedings initiated based on the criminal procedural legislation in force prior to the entry into force of the adversarial accusatory criminal justice system, the accused or defendant may request from the competent jurisdictional body the review of said measures, so that, the judge of the case, in the terms of articles 153 to 171 of the National Code of Criminal Procedures, having given notice to the parties, so that the Public Prosecutor may investigate and prove what is appropriate, and having held the corresponding hearing, the jurisdictional body, taking into consideration the risk assessment, may resolve on the imposition, review, substitution, modification or cessation, in terms of the preventive detention rules of article 19 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, as well as of the National Code of Criminal Procedures. In case of substituting the precautionary measure, it shall apply as appropriate the monitoring thereof in terms of articles 176 to 182 of the cited Code.  

Sixth.- The Attorney General's Office shall propose to the National Public Security Council the achievement of the agreements it deems necessary between the authorities of the federal entities and the federation within the framework of the Federal Law for the Protection of Persons Involved in Criminal Proceedings.  
Mexico City, June 15, 2016.- Sen. Roberto Gil Zuarth, President.- Rep. José de Jesús Zambrano Grijalva, President.- Sen. Hilda Esthela Flores Escalera, Secretary.- Rep. Verónica Delgadillo García, Secretary.- Signatures."

In compliance with the provisions of section I of Article 89 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, and for its due publication and observance, I issue this Decree at the Residence of the Federal Executive Power, in Mexico City, on the sixteenth day of June, two thousand sixteen.- Enrique Peña Nieto.- Signature.- The Minister of the Interior, Miguel Ángel Osorio Chong.-Signature.  

DECREE reforming, adding and repealing various provisions of the Federal Criminal Code on Combating Corruption.  
Published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on July 18, 2016

Sole Article.- The first paragraph and subsection e) of article 201; the title of Title Tenth; the first paragraph of article 212; article 213; article 213 Bis; the title of Chapter II of Title Tenth; the first paragraph and its section III, the second and third paragraphs of article 214; sections VI, IX, XI, XIII and the second and third paragraphs of article 215; the first and second paragraphs of article 216; the title of Chapter V of Title Tenth; the first paragraph, section I and subsections B), C), D), section III and the second paragraph of article 217; the third and fourth paragraphs of article 218; section I and the second paragraph of article 219; section I and the third and fourth paragraphs of article 220; the second paragraph of article 221; sections I, II and the current third and fourth paragraphs of article 222; sections I, II, III and the third and fourth paragraphs of article 223; the current first, second, fourth, fifth and sixth paragraphs of article 224; sections VI, X, XIII, XVII, XX, XXIV, XXVIII and XXXII of article 225 are reformed; a third paragraph with sections I, II, a fourth paragraph, a fifth paragraph with sections I, II, III and IV, a sixth and a seventh paragraph to article 212 are added; a subsection E) to section I, a section I Bis with subsections A) and B) and a second paragraph to article 217; an article 217 Bis; a section IV to article 221; a section III with subsections a, b and a second paragraph renumbering the subsequent ones to article 222; a third paragraph renumbering the subsequent ones to article 224, and the fourth paragraph of article 225 of the Federal Criminal Code is repealed, to read as follows:

……….  
Transitory

First.- This Decree shall enter into force upon the appointment by the Senate of the Republic of the Head of the Specialized Prosecutor's Office on crimes related to acts of corruption, created in terms of the provisions of the second paragraph of the Eighteenth Transitory Article of the Decree reforming, adding and repealing various provisions of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, on political-electoral matters, published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on February 10, 2014, through Agreement A/011/14 creating the Specialized Prosecutor's Office on Crimes related to Acts of Corruption and establishing its powers, published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on March 12, 2014.  
Second.- As of the entry into force of this Decree, in the event that the reforms contained therein contemplate a legal description of a criminal conduct that in the reformed articles was contemplated as a crime and by virtue of the present reforms, is denominated, penalized or aggravated in a different manner, provided that the conducts and facts respond to the description now established, the following shall apply:

I.	In cases of facts that constitute any of the crimes reformed by this Decree, when knowledge thereof is obtained, the Public Prosecutor shall initiate the investigation in accordance with the resulting type translation;  
II.	In investigations initiated, in which criminal action has not yet been exercised, the Public Prosecutor shall exercise it in accordance with the resulting type translation;  
III.	In proceedings instituted, in which accusatory conclusions have not yet been formulated, the Public Prosecutor shall formulate them in accordance with the resulting type translation;  

IV.	In proceedings pending judgment in first and second instance, the judge or the Court, respectively, may effect the type translation in accordance with the conduct that has been proven and its modalities, and
V.	The executing authority when applying any modality of benefit for the sentenced person, shall consider the penalties that have been imposed, based on the type translation, according to the corresponding modalities.  
Third.- Once the General Law to Prevent, Investigate and Punish Torture and Other Cruel, Inhuman or Degrading Treatment or Punishment that establishes subsection a) of section XXI of article 73 enters into force, section XIII of article 215 shall be repealed and proceedings initiated for facts that occur as of said entry into force shall be followed in accordance with the provisions thereof.  
Proceedings initiated before the validity of said law shall continue their processing in accordance with this Code.  
Fourth.- Sentenced persons shall continue serving their sentence in accordance with the provisions of the legislation in force at the time it became final.  
Mexico City, June 17, 2016.- Sen. Roberto Gil Zuarth, President.- Rep. José de Jesús Zambrano Grijalva, President.- Sen. César Octavio Pedroza Gaitán, Secretary.- Rep. Isaura Ivanova Pool Pech, Secretary.- Signatures."

In compliance with the provisions of section I of Article 89 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, and for its due publication and observance, I issue this Decree at the Residence of the Federal Executive Power, in Mexico City, on the eighteenth day of July, two thousand sixteen.- Enrique Peña Nieto.- Signature.- The Minister of the Interior, Miguel Ángel Osorio Chong.-Signature.

DECREE reforming and adding various provisions of the General Health Law and the Federal Penal Code. Published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on June 19, 2017

Second Article.- A final paragraph is added to article 198 of the Federal Penal Code, to read as follows:
………
Transitories

First.- This Decree shall enter into force the day following its publication in the Official Gazette of the Federation. Second.- As of the entry into force of this Decree, the Ministry of Health shall strengthen the programs and actions referred to in Chapter IV, of the Eleventh Title of the General Health Law, with emphasis on the prevention, treatment, rehabilitation, social reintegration and control of the consumption of cannabis sativa, indica and americana or marijuana and its derivatives, by girls, boys and adolescents, as well as the treatment of persons addicted to said narcotics. Third.- The General Health Council, based on the results of national research, shall acknowledge the therapeutic or medicinal value that leads to the production of pharmaceuticals derived from cannabis sativa, indica and americana or marijuana and its derivatives, to guarantee the health of patients. Fourth.- The Ministry of Health shall have 180 days from the entry into force of this Decree, to harmonize the regulations and rules regarding the therapeutic use of TETRAHYDROCANNABINOL of the following isomers: ∆6a (10a), ∆6a (7), ∆7, ∆8, ∆9, ∆10, ∆9 (11) and their stereochemical variants. Mexico City, April 28, 2017.- Sen. Pablo Escudero Morales, President.- Rep. María Guadalupe Murguía Gutiérrez, President.- Sen. Lorena Cuéllar Cisneros, Secretary.- Rep. Raúl Domínguez Rex, Secretary.- Signatures."

DECREE issuing the General Law to Prevent, Investigate and Punish Torture and Other Cruel, Inhuman or Degrading Treatment or Punishment; and reforming, adding and repealing various provisions of the Federal Penal Code, the Law of the National Human Rights Commission, the Law of the National Public Security System and the International Extradition Law. Published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on June 26, 2017

SECOND ARTICLE.- Section V is added to article 85 and section XV of article 215 is reformed; and sections II and XIII of Article 215, as well as section XII of article 225, of the Federal Penal Code, are repealed, to read as follows:
………. Transitories

First. This Decree shall enter into force the day following its publication in the Official Gazette of the Federation. Second. The Federal Law to Prevent and Punish Torture, published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on December 27, 1991, is abrogated. Proceedings initiated for acts occurring as of the entry into force of this Law, shall be continued in accordance with the provisions of the National Code of Criminal Procedures and this Law. Proceedings initiated before the effectiveness of this law shall continue their processing in accordance with the legislation applicable at the time of their initiation. Sentenced persons shall continue serving their sentence in accordance with the provisions of the legislation in force at the time it became final. Those persons, sentenced or prosecuted, whose evidence presented against them lacks probative value, having been obtained directly through torture and any other violation of human or fundamental rights, as well as evidence obtained by legal means but derived from such acts, may file the corresponding appeals and incidents. Third. Within a maximum period of one hundred eighty days counted from the entry into force of this Decree, the legislature of each federative entity must harmonize its legal framework in accordance therewith. Fourth. The authorities of the three levels of government, within the scope of their powers, must adopt and publish the protocols and criteria referred to in the General Law to Prevent, Investigate and Punish Torture and other Cruel, Inhuman or Degrading Treatment or Punishment, within a period of one hundred eighty days following the publication of this Decree. Fifth. The Office of the Attorney General of the Republic shall have a period of one hundred eighty days following the date on which this Decree enters into force, to issue the National Program to Prevent and Punish Torture and other Cruel, Inhuman or Degrading Treatment or Punishment and to have the necessary technological infrastructure to operate the National Registry of the Crime of Torture. Within ninety days following the fulfillment of the period indicated in the preceding paragraph, the prosecutors' offices of the federative entities must launch their corresponding registries. Sixth. The Federation and the federative entities shall have a period of ninety days following the date on which this Decree enters into force, to create and operate their Special Prosecutors' Offices for the investigation of the crime of torture, except in cases where, due to lack of sufficient resources, they must be exercised by the corresponding specialized administrative unit. Seventh. The authorities of the three levels of government, within the scope of their powers, must initiate continuous training programs for their public servants in accordance with the provisions of the General Law to Prevent, Investigate and Punish Torture and Other Cruel, Inhuman or Degrading Treatment or Punishment, within a period of one hundred eighty days following the publication of this Decree. Eighth. The authorities of the three levels of government, within the scope of their powers and within a period not exceeding ninety days from the entry into force of this Decree, must carry out the necessary procedures and perform the legal and administrative acts that are necessary to provide the Institutions of Justice Procurement with the organic and occupational structure necessary for compliance with the Law. Ninth. Within one hundred eighty days following the entry into force of this Decree, the National Human Rights Commission must formally install the National Mechanism for the Prevention of Torture and Other Cruel, Inhuman or Degrading Treatment or Punishment, and issue the bases for its operation and functioning in the ordinary session immediately following the installation. In the same manner, within ninety days following the fulfillment of the period indicated in the preceding paragraph, they must issue the general guidelines that determine the modalities and procedures to be followed during visits. The head of the National Mechanism for Prevention shall make the appointment of the Executive Director within ninety days following the publication of this Decree in the Official Gazette of the Federation. The election of the members of the Technical Committee referred to in section II of article 73 of this Law, shall be made for a single occasion, according to the following graduality:
Of the four expert persons elected, two shall serve for two years and the other two shall serve for four years, a situation that shall be defined by the Senate according to the majority vote; the foregoing so that there is staggered replacement in the composition of the Technical Committee, so that once the two-year period of the members elected for said period concludes, those who replace them shall be elected under the terms of the law for four years. The President of the Technical Committee of the National Mechanism for Prevention shall serve in office for the duration of their term as President of the National Human Rights Commission. Tenth. The expenditures generated as a result of the entry into force of this Decree for the dependencies and entities of the Federal Public Administration, shall be covered with charges to their budgets for the current fiscal year and subsequent ones. Likewise, the federative entities must make the necessary budgetary provisions and adjustments to comply with the obligations established in this Decree. Eleventh. The expenditures generated as a result of the operation of the National Mechanism for the Prevention of Torture for the National Human Rights Commission, shall be covered with charges to its approved budget for the current fiscal year and subsequent ones. Twelfth. The legislatures of the states and the legislative body of Mexico City, under the terms of applicable legislation, must allocate resources for compliance with the obligations incumbent upon them under the terms of this Decree. Thirteenth. In the federative entities where there is no Victims Assistance Commission, the public institutions of the federative entity must provide assistance to Victims in accordance with the provisions of Title Six of the General Law to Prevent, Investigate and Punish Torture and Other Cruel, Inhuman or Degrading Treatment or Punishment. Likewise, in accordance with article 79 of the General Law on Victims, the Executive Commission for Victim Assistance shall be competent to comply with the provisions of the preceding paragraph. In the case established in the first paragraph of this article, the request referred to in section I of article 91 of the Law must be signed by the Secretary of Government of the corresponding federative entity. Fourteenth. Once, under the terms of the provisions of the Fifth Transitory Article of this Decree, the Office of the Attorney General of the Republic begins to operate the National Registry of the Crime of Torture, the Executive Commission and the Institutions of Justice Procurement may enter into collaboration agreements for the transmission of information on Victims of the crime of torture to said Registry. Fifteenth. Within a period not exceeding one hundred eighty days counted from the entry into force of this Decree, the Executive Commission for Victim Assistance must carry out the necessary acts to make the organic modifications that are indispensable for compliance with the provisions thereof. Sixteenth. In order to comply with the powers established in the General Law to Prevent, Investigate and Punish Torture and Other Cruel, Inhuman or Degrading Treatment or Punishment, the Executive Commission for Victim Assistance shall make the adjustments to its Organic Statute and other internal regulations that are necessary, as well as to the trust that administers the resources of the Aid, Assistance and Comprehensive Reparation Fund, within the period of one hundred eighty days counted from its entry into force. Mexico City, April 26, 2017.- Rep. María Guadalupe Murguía Gutiérrez, President.-Sen. Pablo Escudero Morales, President.- Rep. María Eugenia Ocampo Bedolla, Secretary.- Sen. María Elena Barrera Tapia, Secretary.- Signatures."

In compliance with the provisions of section I of Article 89 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States, and for its due publication and observance, I issue this Decree at the Residence of the Federal Executive Power, in Mexico City, on the twenty-first of June, two thousand seventeen.- Enrique Peña Nieto.- Signature.- The Secretary of the Interior, Miguel Ángel Osorio Chong.-Signature. DECREE issuing the General Law on Forced Disappearance of Persons, Disappearance Committed by Private Individuals and the National System for the Search for Persons, and reforming and repealing various provisions of the Federal Penal Code and the General Health Law. Published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on November 17, 2017

SECOND ARTICLE.- The third paragraph of article 25 is reformed; Chapter III Bis of the Tenth Title comprised of articles 215-A to 215-D is repealed, and an article 280 Bis is added to the Seventeenth Title of the Federal Penal Code, to read as follows:

………. Transitories

First. This Decree shall enter into force sixty days after its publication in the Official Gazette of the Federation. As of the entry into force of this Decree and until the issuance of the instruments referred to in the Fourteenth Transitory Article, the Prosecutor's Office and the Local Prosecutors' Offices and other authorities must comply with the search obligations in accordance with the ordinances that have been issued previously, provided they do not conflict with this Law. The Prosecutor's Office and the Local Prosecutors' Offices, in addition to the protocols provided for in this Law, shall continue to apply existing protocols for the search for persons in vulnerable situations. Second. The Law of the National Registry of Data on Missing or Disappeared Persons is abrogated. Third. The Specialized Prosecutors' Offices and the National Search Commission shall begin operations within thirty days following the entry into force of this Decree. Within thirty days following the National Search Commission beginning its functions, it must issue the guiding protocols for its operation provided for in article 53, section VIII, of this Law. Within one hundred eighty days following the National Search Commission entering into operation, it must issue the National Search Program. The public servants who comprise the Specialized Prosecutors' Offices and the Search Commissions must be certified within the year following their creation. For the purposes of the provisions of the preceding paragraph, the National Search Commission shall issue the criteria provided for in article 53, section L, of this Law, within ninety days following its entry into operation. The National Search Commission and the local commissions may, from the time they enter into operation, exercise the powers that this Law confers upon them in relation to search processes that are pending. The National Search Commission shall coordinate the search for disappeared persons related to searches in which, upon the entry into force of this Law, federal authorities participate. Fourth. The Local Search Commissions must enter into operation within ninety days following the entry into force of this Decree. The National Search Commission must provide the necessary advice to the federal entities for the establishment of their Local Search Commissions. Fifth. The Citizens Council must be formed within ninety days following the entry into force of this Decree. Within thirty days following its formation, the Citizens Council must issue its operating rules. Sixth. The National System for the Search for Persons must be installed within one hundred eighty days following the publication of this Decree. In the first ordinary session of the National Search System, the guidelines and models referred to in article 49, sections I, VIII, XV and XVI of this Law must be issued. In the second ordinary session of the National Search System, which is held in accordance with the provisions of this Law, the certification and specialization criteria provided for in article 55 must be issued. Seventh. Within one hundred eighty days following the issuance of the guidelines provided for in the preceding transitory article, the National Search Commission must have the necessary technological infrastructure and begin to operate the National Registry of Disappeared and Not Located Persons. Within ninety days following the commencement of operation of the National Registry of Disappeared and Not Located Persons, the Federative Entities must launch their registries of Disappeared and Not Located Persons. Eighth. While the registries of Disappeared and Not Located Persons begin to operate, the Local Prosecutors' Offices must incorporate into a provisional registry, electronic or printed, the information from the Reports, Complaints or Notices received in accordance with the provisions of article 106 of this Law. The Federation and the Federative Entities must migrate the information contained in the provisional registries referred to in the preceding paragraph, within fifteen days following the commencement of operation of the registries of Disappeared and Not Located Persons. Ninth. The Congress of the Union must legislate on the Special Declaration of Absence within one hundred eighty days following the date on which this Decree enters into force. The Federative Entities must issue and, where appropriate, harmonize the legislation that corresponds to their scope of competence within one hundred eighty days following the date on which this Decree enters into force. In those Federative Entities where the harmonization provided for in the Third Chapter of the Fourth Title of this Law has not been carried out, within the period indicated in the preceding paragraph, the provisions of said Chapter shall be applicable notwithstanding the provisions of the applicable local legislation. Tenth. As of the entry into force of this Decree, in the event that the provisions contained therein contemplate the legal description of conduct provided for in other norms as

crimes and by virtue of this Law they are denominated, typified, penalized or aggravated in a different manner, provided that the conduct and facts correspond to the description now established, the following shall apply:  
I. In cases of acts that constitute any of the crimes of this Law, when knowledge thereof is obtained, the Public Prosecutor's Office shall initiate the investigation in accordance with this Law; II. In investigations initiated in which criminal action has not yet been exercised, the Public Prosecutor's Office shall exercise it in accordance with the type translation that is appropriate; III. In proceedings initiated under the mixed criminal system in which the Public Prosecutor's Office has not yet formulated accusatory conclusions, it shall proceed to their preparation and presentation in accordance with the type translation that, where appropriate, is appropriate; IV. In proceedings initiated under the adversarial accusatory system, in which the Public Prosecutor's Office has not yet presented an accusation, it shall proceed to its preparation and presentation according to the type translation that may be appropriate; V. In proceedings pending judgment in first and second instance, the judge or the corresponding Court may effect the type translation in accordance with the conduct that has been proven, including its modalities, without exceeding the amount of the penalties indicated in the respective law in force at the time of the commission of the acts, and

VI. The executing authority, when applying any modality of benefit for the sentenced person, shall consider the penalties that have been imposed, according to the corresponding modalities. Eleventh. The Federal Executive, within a period of one hundred eighty days from the entry into force of this Decree, must issue and harmonize the regulatory provisions that correspond in accordance with the provisions of this Decree. Twelfth. Within thirty days following the creation of the National Search Commission, the Executive Secretariat of the National Public Security System shall transfer to it the technological tools and information it has collected in compliance with the provisions of the Law of the National Registry of Data on Missing or Disappeared Persons. Within ninety days following receipt of the information referred to in the preceding paragraph, the National Search Commission must transmit to the Specialized Prosecutors' Offices the information on Disappeared or Not Located Persons that correspond to the scope of their competence. The Specialized Prosecutors' Offices must update the content of the National Registry, as follows:  
I. Within one hundred eighty days following receipt of the information, the corresponding Specialized Prosecutor's Office must collect information on the persons registered in the Registry provided for in the Law of the National Registry of Data on Missing or Disappeared Persons that correspond to its scope of competence, so that said information is in accordance with the provisions of article 106 and, where appropriate, article 112 of this Law; II. Under the terms of the preceding section, the Specialized Prosecutors' Offices that are materially prevented from updating the information within the provided period, must publish a register with the name of the Disappeared or Not Located Persons whose information has not been updated, so that,

within the following one hundred twenty days, the Relatives and civil society organizations provide the information that could be useful to carry out said update; III. Once the information is updated, the National Search Commission must enter it into the corresponding registry, except that the update reveals that the person was located, in which case, it shall be recorded in the National Registry of Disappeared Persons, and
IV. Having carried out the action provided for in section II of this article, if the registry has not been updated, the corresponding Specialized Prosecutor's Office shall be materially unable to update it. In this case, the registry shall remain with the notation of pending update and shall be migrated, with that character, to the corresponding registry. Thirteenth. The National Forensic Data Bank, the Federal forensic registries and those of the Federative Entities shall begin to operate within the year following the entry into force of this Decree. Within three months following the commencement of operation of said registries, the authorities that possess forensic information must incorporate it into the corresponding registry. Fourteenth. Within one hundred eighty days following the entry into force of this Decree, the National Conference on Justice Procurement must issue the Standardized Investigation Protocol referred to in article 99 of this Law. Fifteenth. The authorities and institutions that collect the information referred to in article 103 must incorporate it within a period of one year from the entry into force of this Decree. Sixteenth. In the Federative Entities where there is no Victims Assistance Commission, the competent public institutions of the Federative Entity must provide assistance to Victims in accordance with the provisions of Title Four of this Law. Seventeenth. The expenditures generated as a result of the entry into force of this Decree for the dependencies and parastatal entities of the Federal Public Administration, shall be covered with the resources approved by the Chamber of Deputies in the Expenditure Budget of the Federation for the fiscal year in question. The Legislatures of the Federative Entities, under the terms of applicable legislation, must allocate resources for compliance with the obligations incumbent upon them under the terms of this Decree. Eighteenth. The guidelines for determining the techniques and procedures to be applied for the preservation of corpses or remains of persons referred to in article 130 of this Law must be published in the Official Gazette of the Federation within the period of one hundred eighty days from the entry into force of this Law. Nineteenth. The Office of the Attorney General of the Republic must issue the necessary technological guidelines to ensure that the registries and the National Forensic Data Bank have the appropriate technical characteristics and technological support, in accordance with the provisions of articles 131, section III and 132, within the period of one hundred eighty days from the entry into force of this Law. Within the period provided for in the preceding paragraph, the Office of the Attorney General of the Republic shall issue the necessary guidelines so that the authorities of the different levels of government submit in a standardized manner the information that will be integrated into the National Registry of Deceased Persons Not Identified and Not Claimed and the National Forensic Data Bank provided for in the General Law on

Forced Disappearance of Persons, Disappearance committed by Private Individuals and the National System for the Search for Persons. Twentieth. While the Federative Entities are in the process of integrating their Search Commissions, the obligations provided for these Commissions in the Law shall be assumed by the Secretary of Government of each entity. Likewise, the Federative Entities must make the necessary budgetary provisions and adjustments to comply with the obligations established in this Decree. Twenty-first. Within one hundred eighty days following the entry into force of this Decree, the National Search Commission must issue the guidelines referred to in section XIV of article 53 of the Law. Mexico City, October 12, 2017.- Sen. Ernesto Cordero Arroyo, President.- Rep. Jorge Carlos Ramírez Marín, President.- Sen. Lorena Cuéllar Cisneros, Secretary.- Rep. Ana Guadalupe Perea Santos, Secretary.- Signatures."

DECREE adding an article 190 Bis to the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law, and an article 168 ter to the Federal Penal Code.  
Published in the Official Gazette of the Federation on January 24, 2020

Article Second. An article 168 ter is added to the Federal Penal Code, to read as follows:

………
Transitories

First. This Decree shall enter into force the day following its publication in the Official Gazette of the Federation.  
Second. Private individuals who possess devices or equipment that serve to block, cancel or nullify cellular telephone, radiocommunication or data or image transmission signals, must deliver them to the Secretariat of Security and Citizen Protection, destroy or, as the case may be, exclude from the country, within 30 days from the entry into force of this Decree.  
Third. The Secretariat of Security and Citizen Protection shall coordinate and supervise the delivery-receipt by private individuals of all devices whose purpose is to block, cancel or nullify cellular telephone, radiocommunication or data or image transmission signals and shall proceed to render them unusable under the methods it deems appropriate.  
Fourth. The corresponding authorities shall have a term of no more than 180 calendar days to make modifications to Official Mexican Standard 220-SCFI-2017, in order to harmonize it with the contents of this Decree.  
Fifth. The Secretariat of Security and Citizen Protection shall report annually on compliance with the provisions expressed in this Decree, to the Public Security Commissions of the Senate of the Republic and the federal Chamber of Deputies.  
Mexico City, December 11, 2019.- Rep. Laura Angélica Rojas Hernández, President.-Sen. Mónica Fernández Balboa, President.- Rep. Lizbeth Mata Lozano, Secretary.- Sen. Primo Dothé Mata, Secretary.- Signatures."

………
Transitories

First. This Decree shall enter into force the day following its publication in the Official Gazette of the Federation.  
Second. All provisions that oppose the content of this Decree are repealed.  
Third. Criminal proceedings that are being processed upon the entry into force of this Decree shall continue in accordance with the provisions in force at the time of the commission of the acts.  

Persons who have committed a crime contemplated in this Decree prior to its entry into force shall be subject to the provisions in force at the time it was committed.  
Fourth. Sentenced persons shall continue to serve their sentence in accordance with the provisions established in the legislation in force at the time it became final.  
Fifth. The application of the rules in the criminal cases referred to in this Decree shall be made in accordance with the provisions of article 14 of the Political Constitution of the United Mexican States.  
Mexico City, February 18, 2021.- Rep. Dulce María Sauri Riancho, President.- Sen. Oscar Eduardo Ramírez Aguilar, President.- Rep. Julieta Macías Rábago, Secretary.- Sen. Lilia Margarita Valdez Martínez, Secretary.- Signatures."
